(2) *Inner tangential row:* the sea **deepens the brake** — $\tau_I$ at seed $-0.243$ dressed vs $-0.218$ bare (the sea adds $-0.026$ backward, against the $+0.032$ forward estimate); along the release it deepens to $-0.51$ by $t\approx1.25$ and never lifts toward $0$. (3) *Metabolism ledger:* **the loop runs backward** — net sea torque at seed $-0.082$ (estimate $+0.11$ forward); per-layer $(I,M,O)=(-0.026,-0.031,+0.012)$ vs estimate $(+0.032,\sim0,+0.031)$. (4) *Support rows:* **the radial ledger opens, not closes** — sea radial supply $(I,M,O)=(-13.7\%,+7.2\%,-38.0\%)$ vs the FCC estimate $+6.6\%$ mean; dressed support $(0.822,0.952,0.600)$ vs bare $(0.960,0.880,0.980)$. **Root cause, isolated (five-variant cross-check on the estimate kernel).** The run kernel reproduces the published Section 42 rows exactly under the estimate's own convention (6 axial sites, uniform $2R/c_f$ loop delay: radial mean $+5.3\%$, $\tau_I+0.029$). The true FCC-12 angular placement at that same convention nearly *doubles* the margins ($+14.8\%$, $\tau_I+0.052$) — the Section 43 count-weighted "FCC" sum used 6-axial placement and was, if anything, conservative on geometry. Replacing the uniform loop delay with **exact per-pair causal delays flips both ledgers** (radial mean $-17.5\%$, net torque backward; the $\gamma=2\omega$ relaxation does not rescue it). The dressed budget's confinement lived in the uniform-delay idealization: per-pair loop phases spread by $\pm\rho_a/c_f$ per leg across the braid's layers, sweeping more than a full commensurability band (band period $\approx2.1$ in $R$). An exact-delay spacing scan finds the band structure survives but shifts inward: confining at $R\approx3.0$–$3.5$ (at $3.5$: radial $+8.6\%$, $\tau_I$ **$+0.114$ forward $=52\%$ of the $0.218$ need**) and again at $5.5$–$6.5$; the named $4.25$ sits near the anti-confining trough. **Release phenomenology.** Same first-blocker shape as Row 2 (inner brake spiral: I bleeds, contracts $0.462\to0.27$, whips outward through the rail); canonical halt $t=9.57$ (M$+$ radius runaway), chart halt $t=11.5$ (M$+$ speed runaway) after $2127$ escapement transactions. The chart booking shows the **first native inward middle slip** on record ($r_M$ $1.0\to0.63$ before the general dispersal takes it) — the dressed middle's wake-equilibrium pull, a durable observation for the Section 32–35 rail mechanics. Cap fall-in stays cured. Twin row: $10^{-3}$ kick grows to $0.22$ by $t=6.3$ — no return basin on the dispersing base.

**Operator decision (same thread, 2026-07-09): REJECTED**, per the run recommendation — first blocker `sea_anti_confines_at_exact_causal_delays` (seed-grade: the declared environment does not supply the estimated budget once the uniform-loop-delay idealization is replaced by exact causal delays; shape loss then proceeds on the Row 2 clock and blocker). The gate stays fail-closed (`retainedBranchClaim=false`, `scoreMovement=no_score_increase`). Durable findings: exact-delay promotion is a GATE for estimate-grade sea results — uniform-loop-delay estimates must carry per-pair causal delays before tabling; the exact-delay confining band sits at $R\approx3.25$–$3.5$ where the forward inner torque reaches half the need, moving the SH-0-sea supply$=$deficit fixed-point question to that spacing (or to sea self-structure that re-tunes the band); the first native inward middle slip; and the responsive-sea environment (retained orientation history, settle discipline, metabolism rows, order-parameter rows) as reusable machinery on the owner script.

Next closure goal: re-run the exact-delay dressed budget as an estimate-grade instrument row (the five-variant cross-check promoted to an owner script with tests), locate the exact-delay supply$=$deficit fixed point over spacing and sea structure, and only then consider a Row 4 tabling — no candidate row should be tabled from a uniform-loop-delay estimate again.

(2) *Inner tangential row:* **the expectation is hit exactly and sustained** — $\tau_I$ at seed $-0.1006$ dressed (expectation $\sim-0.10$; bare $-0.218$; Row 3 deepened it to $-0.243$); holds $\approx-0.10$ through $t\approx0.5$, deepens only to $-0.175$ (vs $-0.51$ at the same epoch in Row 3); the sea's forward feed on the inner is steady at $+0.117$–$0.13$ across the whole coherent phase, and $\beta_I$ decays $0.455\to0.358$ by $t=1$ (vs $0.242$ in Row 3). **The Rows 1–3 inner-brake invariant ($-0.22$, knob-rigid, Section 41) is breakable by environment — first native demonstration.** (3) *Metabolism ledger:* **net sea torque positive, as predicted** — seed $+0.0285$, growing along the release ($+0.23$ by $t=1.5$); per-layer at seed I $+0.117$ (expectation $\sim+0.12$), M $-0.052$, O $+0.104$. (4) *Support rows:* **the radial surplus never arrived.** Native sea radial supply $(I,M,O)=(-4.9\%,-9.8\%,+18.1\%)$, mean $+1.2\%$ vs the $\sim+10\%$ tabled expectation. Isolated at fixed geometry by the cross-check: fast-alignment matches the packet; the declared $\gamma=2\omega$ relaxation collapses the radial mean to $-5.8\%$ while the tangential row is lag-robust ($+0.122$ at every grade). ## 45. Exact-Delay Correction on the Evaluator Stack: the Row 3 Blocker Reproduced, the Band Relocated, and the Fixed Point Found Near R* ≈ 3.4 — the Named Spacing Question Inverts (2026-07-09)

(Fresh number under the numbering notice; the Row 3 native run record is Section 44, cite by title.) The Row 3 rejection's first blocker `sea_anti_confines_at_exact_causal_delays` promoted to this stack's owner instrument: `saturationMarginPerP0` now defaults to EXACT per-(receiver, sea-site) causal delays on the return leg (the sea dipole's orientation read at its true emission time $t-r_j/c_f$ per receiver, braid sources exactly causally delayed inside); the uniform $2R/c_f$ idealization is retained as `exactDelays:false` regression mode with the published pre-Row-3 rows pinned in tests.

**Result 1 — the blocker reproduces exactly on this stack.** At $a=4.25$: uniform mode $+4.2\%$ radial mean $\to$ exact mode $\mathbf{-9.7\%}$ (anti-confining), inner torque $0.032\to0.007$, middle torque flips backward. The idealization error is structural, not numerical: the braid radius ($\sim1.24$) is order-one against the shell radius, so per-pair loop phases spread by $\sim2\omega\cdot\Delta r\approx2.5$ rad — averaging a phase-critical quantity over that spread was never legitimate. Sections 40/42/43's named-spacing claims and brainstorm entry 31's "4.25 fixed point" speculation are REFUTED at exact-delay grade (uniform-mode rows remain pinned as regression history).

**Result 2 — the band relocates inward and the fixed point exists.** Exact-delay sweep (6-shell, fast, $p_0=2.20$): $R=2.75$: $+44\%$; $3.0$: $+24\%$; $3.25$: $+12.8\%$; $3.5$: $+2.9\%$; $3.75$–$4.5$: anti-confining. The supply$=$deficit point (6% mean): $R^*\approx3.42$ at 6-shell, $\approx3.5$ at FCC-12 (doubling). Inner tangential torque in the band: $+0.066$ at $3.25$ (6-shell) — $\approx$ half the $0.218$ need at FCC-12, cross-validating the native run's independent numbers. Two stacks now agree at exact-delay grade.

**Result 3 — the question inverts.** A homogeneous sea of v1-cadence braids equilibrates (at this estimate grade) at spacing $R^*\approx3.4$–$3.5$, NOT at the named $4.25$. Either: (a) the named sea-spacing constant revises toward $\sim3.4$ — a PREDICTION of this program against the SH-0-sea lane's provenance for $4.25$ (that provenance needs auditing: whose assembly, whose cadence?); or (b) real sea assemblies differ from v1 (cadence/geometry), shifting THEIR dressing band — the commensurability scaling means the band position tracks the sea assemblies' own phases, so a mixed sea is not required to sit at this braid's band. Discriminating (a) vs (b) is the named question handed jointly to SH-0-sea and the Row 4 design; no Row 4 tabling before it is answered (concurring with the native thread's prerequisite).

Fail-closed: `retainedBranchClaim=false`, `scoreMovement=no_score_increase`; estimate grade (single/FCC shells, total saturation, dipole back-field, $N_t=8$ wobble).

Next closure goal: audit the provenance of the named spacing $4.25$ (SH-0-sea record), and run the exact-delay fixed-point solve over sea-assembly cadence (does a sea whose assemblies run at $\approx0.8\,\omega$ put its dressing band at $4.25$? — the commensurability inversion that would reconcile the constant), as the Row 4 prerequisite.

## 46. The 4.25 Provenance Audit and the Cadence Solve: the Named Spacing Was a Window-Midpoint Convention, No Dynamics Rescues It, and the Constant Should Revise to R* ≈ 3.4 (2026-07-09)

Row 4 prerequisite, both halves executed.

**Half 1 — provenance (the operator did not recall; the record does).** The named spacing traces to the SH-0-sea dipole-wake-sum diagnostic ([sh-0-sea-diagnostic-candidate-model.md], "static-held run"): a retention WINDOW $a_{\mathrm{FCC}}\in[3,\,5.35]$ was computed (amplitude-only inward-response floor crossing, far-field $\sim a^{-5}$), and $4.25$ was selected as *"the eligible crossing row nearest the window midpoint."* Three provenance facts matter: (i) $4.25$ is a **selection convention, not a derived equilibrium**; (ii) the diagnostic was amplitude-only (no phase structure — the delayed-echo variant was the named follow-up); (iii) the source row was the OLD two-speed candidate (`two-speed-preferred-row:u0.8:v0.2`), not the spindle. There is NO computed result anywhere in the record that pins $4.25$ as a physical point.

**Half 2 — the cadence/dynamics solve: the band is geometric and immovable.** Exact-delay relaxation sweep at $a=4.25$, $\gamma/\omega\in\{0.5,1,2,5,10\}$: radial mean stays anti-confining at every rate ($-2.6\%$ to $-9.9\%$; test-pinned). The sea's response DYNAMICS cannot move the band — its position is set by the braid's cadence and the loop geometry (the exact per-pair phases), which no orientation model reaches. The "$0.8\,\omega$ sea" reconciliation is therefore dead at this grade: within this instrument's physics there is no declared sea parameter that makes $4.25$ confining for a v1-cadence braid.

**Resolution — no conflict, a refinement.** The exact-delay fixed point $R^*\approx3.4$ sits INSIDE the legacy retention window $[3,5.35]$, toward its lower edge; the old amplitude-only rows and the new phase-aware rows are mutually consistent everywhere they overlap (legacy $\Pi_R$ strongly inward at $3.0$; ours $+24\%$ there). The correction is to the midpoint CONVENTION only. **Recommendation (operator decision required — canon-adjacent named constant): revise the named sea-spacing candidate from $a_{\mathrm{FCC}}=4.25$ to the supply$=$deficit fixed point $R^*\approx3.4$** (v1-cadence homogeneous sea, exact-delay estimate grade), and re-derive per assembly family whenever the sea's constituent changes. Consequences if adopted: sea number density rises $\approx2\times$; the Row 4 responsive-sea release re-tables at $a=3.4$ where (by construction) the radial ledger closes and the in-band inner torque reaches $\approx$ half the tangential need — the dispersal clock should finally move, which is Row 4's decisive diagnostic.

Fail-closed: `retainedBranchClaim=false`, `scoreMovement=no_score_increase`; the revision is a recommendation, not an edit — the named constant is only re-declared on operator direction.

Next closure goal: operator decision on the spacing revision; if adopted, table Candidate Row 4 (responsive-sea-dressed release of v1 at $a=3.4$, exact-delay-validated environment, same machinery and diagnostics as Row 3).

## 47. The Joint Lag-Robustness Map: Scoped Negative — the FCC Orientational-Dipole Sea Cannot Feed the Middle, in Any Cell; the Family Closes at Its Declared Scope (2026-07-09)

(Fresh number under the numbering notice; the Row 4 run record is one of the two sections numbered 45, cite by title.) Executing the Row 4 gate (both threads' stated prerequisite for any Row 5): the joint $(\gamma/\omega,\ a)$ map on the exact-delay instrument at settled-relaxation grade, both channels at once.

**Instrument correction first.** The relax mode's Euler orientation step at $N_t=8$ is marginally unstable at $\gamma=2\omega$ ($\gamma\,\Delta t\approx1.6$); the map runs at $N_t\ge16$ ($\gamma\,\Delta t\le0.8$), where the rows settle. At settled grade the instrument's relax rows are directionally consistent with the Row 4 native finding (fast $+5.2\%$ $\to$ relax $+2.3\%$ mean at $a=3.4$, 6-shell; the native true-geometry collapse was deeper) — the lag-collapse of the radial channel reproduces at estimate grade.

**The map's verdict is not about the mean — it is about the middle, and it is universal.** Across every cell of $(a\in\{3.0,3.25,3.4\},\ \gamma/\omega\in\{2,5,10\})$: the middle's sea radial row is NEGATIVE ($-6\%$ to $-14\%$), while the outer is fed lavishly ($+12\%$ to $+25\%$) and the inner tangential feed is forward and lag-robust everywhere ($+0.05$ to $+0.10$, confirming Row 4's channel-dependence finding). The geometry escape hatch fails too: sweeping the middle tilt $\alpha_M\in[-30°,+30°]$ moves $\mathrm{rad}_M$ monotonically ($-7.7\%\to-1.2\%$) but never through zero, and the mean collapses at the extremes. **Scoped negative, per the Row 5 gate: within the FCC-12 orientational-dipole sea family at $p_0=2.20$ with declared relaxation dynamics and exact delays, no $(\gamma, a, \alpha_M)$ cell delivers non-negative middle radial supply.** The family cannot fix the layer Row 4 proved is the killer. Pinned in tests.

**What survives, and where the routes now point.** The sea family's durable capabilities: the inner tangential feed (breaks the inner-brake invariant, natively demonstrated) and generous outer radial supply — it feeds the caps and the inner's spin; it starves the equatorial rail layer, structurally (an axial-ish aligned sea field at an equatorial ring). Routes beyond the closed scope, ranked: (a) **complementary allocation** — invert the braid's internal allocation (the Section 37 sum rule makes it free: $\lambda<1$ members over-support the middle bare, e.g. $0.95$ at $\lambda=0.95$) and let the sea feed what it naturally feeds (caps, inner tangential); requires recomputing the sea rows per $\lambda$-geometry since $\mathrm{rad}_M<0$ subtracts regardless — the open question is whether a $\lambda$ exists where bare-M-surplus exceeds the sea's M-penalty while the caps' deficits stay within the sea's O-generosity; (b) **positional/breathing sea response** (a phonon channel — shell radius modulating at the braid cadence feeds the equator in a way orientation cannot); (c) **sea quadrupole/near-field response** (assemblies are not point dipoles at $3$ braid radii). Fail-closed: `retainedBranchClaim=false`, `scoreMovement=no_score_increase`.

Next closure goal: the complementary-allocation solve — joint sweep of the braid's $\lambda$-allocation against the sea rows recomputed per geometry, seeking a cell where every layer's (bare $+$ sea) radial total $\ge1$ at settled-relaxation exact-delay grade with the tangential feed intact; a jointly-closing cell tables Row 5, and a second scoped negative sends the program to the positional-sea channel.

## 48. The Complementary-Allocation Solve: Second Scoped Negative — the Orientational Sea Family Is Closed on Both Axes; the Program Moves to the Positional-Sea Channel (2026-07-09)

Executing Section 47's named route (a): $\lambda$-allocated braids (bare middle surplus via the Section 37 sum-rule freedom) against the sea rows recomputed per geometry (geometry-matched $p_0$, settled relaxation, exact delays, FCC-12 scaling).

**Result — allocation loses to the penalty, everywhere.** Sweep $\lambda\in\{0.85,0.9,0.95\}\times a\in\{3.25,3.4\}$: the bare middle surplus tops out at $0.96$ ($\lambda=0.85$) while the sea's middle penalty at the matched $p_0$ ($2.3$–$2.6$) runs $-0.13$ to $-0.17$ in every cell — total middle stalls at $0.76$–$0.83$, and the min-over-layers never beats $0.80$. That is WORSE than bare v1's $0.88$: for the radial ledger, this sea family is a net liability at every allocation, because it taxes the middle regardless of who holds the surplus and over-feeds the outer past $1.4$ where the surplus is wasted. Pinned in tests (10/10 on the instrument). **Second scoped negative: the FCC orientational-dipole sea at geometry-matched $p_0$ is closed on both the direct axis (Section 47) and the allocation axis.** Per Section 47's ruling, the program moves to the positional-sea channel.

**What the closed family leaves behind (the durable inventory).** Natively demonstrated: the inner tangential feed (breaks the $-0.22$ invariant, sustains at the predicted rate) and positive net sea torque at in-band spacing. Estimate-grade: the retardation-resonance band structure; the channel-dependent lag sensitivity; the exact-delay discipline (binding on all future sea rows). The orientational sea is the braid's angular-momentum bath — that half of the metabolism stands. What it cannot be is the braid's radial scaffold: orientation waves cannot push on the equator.

**The positional-sea channel, framed.** The sea sites' POSITIONS respond (breathing/phonon modes: shell radius and site displacements modulating at the braid's cadence, causally delayed) — a monopole-order density response, coupling to the equatorial ring the way orientation cannot (an in-phase breathing shell modulates the distance, hence the $1/r^2$ supply, at the receiver's own frequency: parametric radial feed). New instrument required (declared site-motion model, exact delays from the start, both channels + the M row as the first readout); the orientational channel remains in the environment as the tangential feed. Fail-closed: `retainedBranchClaim=false`, `scoreMovement=no_score_increase`.

Next closure goal: build the positional-sea (breathing-shell) margin instrument — declared radial site-motion response at the braid cadence, exact per-pair delays, settled grade, the middle's radial row as the decisive first readout — with the orientational tangential feed retained in the model; a positive M row at any declared cell re-opens Row 5 design.

## 49. The Positional-Sea (Breathing-Shell) Margin Instrument: Third Scoped Negative — the Band-Limited Monopole Breathing Response Cannot Feed the Middle at Sub-Field Slew, at Any Phase, at Any Declared Cell (2026-07-09)

Executing Section 48's next closure goal on a new owner instrument ([positional-sea-breathing-margin-instrument.mjs](../../../../scripts/braid-ideal/positional-sea-breathing-margin-instrument.mjs), tests 7 passing): the sea sites' positions respond radially at the braid's cadence — the monopole-order parametric radial feed — with the orientational tangential feed retained in the model and exact per-pair causal delays from the start (the uniform-loop-delay idealization is not implemented in this instrument at all).

**Declared model (nothing fit).** Six-direction shell at spacing $a$ (FCC-12 as the count-scaled $\times2$ row, the stack's convention). Orientational channel retained unchanged from Sections 43–47, including its nearest-sample lookup, so the $\varepsilon=0$ relax-orientation baseline reproduces the parent instrument's exact-delay rows to $<2\times10^{-3}$ (the anchor; tested). Positional channel: radial displacement $s_j$ with first-order relaxation $\dot s_j=\gamma_b(s^{\mathrm{tgt}}_j-s_j)$ toward the saturable target $s^{\mathrm{tgt}}_j=\varepsilon\,a\,W_j(t)$, where $W_j$ is the band-limited (cadence harmonics $1..4$, declared mode physics), cycle-mean-subtracted radial component of the exactly causally delayed softened braid field at the site, normalized to unit cycle max. $\varepsilon$ is the declared fractional breathing amplitude; $\varepsilon<0$ is the declared anti-phase cell; a prescribed-phase scan (drive waveform time-shifted, relative site phasing preserved) bounds the best-phase feed — the model-independent ceiling of the response family. Return leg: emission time solved per pair with the site displaced at its own emission time; moving-site source branch factor $c_f/D_s$; sub-field slew guard on $\max|\dot s|$ (booking validity).

**Three instrument-honesty findings (durable).** (i) *The caustic fan is the parent's dt drift, diagnosed:* the rail layer ($\beta_M=1$) sweeps a caustic fan across external points, so the bare $c_f/D_s$ field at a sea site has integrable spikes whose cycle statistics are Nt-fragile — this is the mechanism behind the orientational instrument's radial-row drift with $N_t$ (its radM sign is Nt-robust; the magnitude is not). An unfiltered positional response to that spike content demands superluminal site slew and voids the return-leg root solve (probed: Nt-divergent); the band limit is therefore not a convenience but the boundary of the representable response family. (ii) *The claim-bearing positional quantity is the frozen-frame delta:* baseline and dressed rows measured with each site's dipole frozen at its settled cycle-mean direction, differenced in the same call — dt-halving-witnessed stable ($<10^{-3}$ across $N_t=32/64/128$) and linear at small $\varepsilon$ (tested). (iii) *Axis sites carry no radial cadence drive:* a point on the rotation axis sees a time-constant radial field under rigid rotation, so only the four equatorial sites breathe — the braid's own cadence signal cannot drive polar breathing at all (tested).

**Validation cross-checks.** The composite booking (orientational settled-relax baseline $+$ frozen-frame positional delta) reproduces the known allocations: at $a=4.25$ the M row is positive ($+0.022$) while min-layer support collapses ($0.75$–$0.79$) — the Row 3 native record's rejected out-of-band allocation; at $a=3.4$ dressed supports land $0.972/0.822/1.063$ (I/M/O) — the Row 4 native M-starves/O-drowns shape ($0.911/0.783/1.166$) at estimate grade.

**The decisive readout — the middle's row.** Across the declared map ($a\in\{3.0,3.25,3.4,4.25\}$, $\gamma_b/\omega\in\{2,5,10\}$ plus the fast limit, $\varepsilon=\pm0.1$, settled grade, 32 cells): the positional delta on the middle is at most $+0.0019$; the phase-scan ceiling is $\le+0.0025$ per $p_0$ at $\varepsilon=0.1$ at every spacing in the band (slope $\le0.025$ per unit $\varepsilon$). The sub-field slew guard caps the declared amplitude at $\varepsilon\lesssim0.125$ (slew per unit $\varepsilon$ $\approx8\,c_f$), so the channel's ceiling at valid booking is $\approx+0.0017$ against the orientational middle hole $0.048$–$0.12$: **shortfall $\ge28\times$ at every in-band spacing** ($35\times$ at $a=3.4$). $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{req}}$ merely to bring the sea's M row to zero is $\approx4.4$ (site slew $\approx35\,c_f$); to reach dressed middle support $1$, $\approx13$. No declared cell produces a positional M rescue and no cell closes min-layer support ($\ge0.97$); verdict `no_positional_m_rescue_in_declared_cells` (test-pinned). **Third scoped negative: within the declared response family — saturable, band-limited, causally-delayed radial site motion driven by the braid's own cadence signal — the breathing shell cannot feed the equatorial rail layer.**

**Why, structurally (durable).** Two findings sharpen Section 48's framing. First, the frozen-orientation baseline has NO middle hole ($+0.0004$): the orientational M starvation is carried entirely by the orientation *waves* — the mean orientation geometry is M-neutral, so the sea's M-penalty is a dynamic-response cost, not a static-field cost. Second, the parametric response lands O-heavy again ($|\Delta_O|$ up to $0.05$ at $\varepsilon=0.1$, versus $|\Delta_M|\le0.002$): with the site dipoles saturated along the braid's axial-ish field, distance modulation modulates an axially-polarized source — the equatorial ring stays out of reach. The sea family's structural signature persists across both channels: it feeds the caps and starves the rail.

**Scope boundary.** Closed here: orientation response (Sections 47–48) and band-limited monopole positional response (this section), both at declared saturation and exact delays. Remaining sea routes at estimate grade, per the Section 47 ranking: quadrupole/near-field response (assemblies are not point dipoles at three braid radii), and sea self-structure beyond response models (per-site phase freedom — a standing phonon lattice rather than a driven response — which is a formation-history burden, not a response calculation). The alternative reading hardens: the sea is the braid's angular-momentum bath and cap support; the equatorial radial scaffold must come from braid-internal structure (the item-25 tangential-closure/I$\to$M-transfer geometry track).

Fail-closed: `retainedBranchClaim=false`, `scoreMovement=no_score_increase`; estimate grade (6-site shell with count scaling, declared response family, frozen-frame delta discipline).

Next closure goal: operator decision on the sea program's fork — (a) run the quadrupole/near-field response row on this instrument's environment (the last response-family cell), or (b) close the sea-as-radial-scaffold question at scoped-negative and return the radial ledger to braid-internal geometry (extend the item-25 objective with per-layer tangential residuals and dynamically sustained I$\to$M transfer), with the sea retained as tangential feed and cap support in all future candidate rows.

## 50. The Quadrupole/Near-Field Response Cell: Fourth Scoped Negative and Family-Complete Closure — the Finite Pair Feeds the Caps Threefold-to-Eightfold and Leaves the Equatorial Hole Untouched; the Sea Is Not the Braid's Radial Scaffold (2026-07-09)

Executing the Section 49 operator ruling, route (a): the last response-family cell — sea assemblies treated as extended objects rather than point dipoles at three braid radii. Owner instrument extended in place ([positional-sea-breathing-margin-instrument.mjs](../../../../scripts/braid-ideal/positional-sea-breathing-margin-instrument.mjs), `returnModel: "finitePair"`; tests 9 passing).

**Declared model.** The saturated dipole realized as its physical antipodal polarity pair: unit-polarity monopoles at $X_j\pm(d/2)\hat p_j$ with $d=p_0=2.1979$ (geometry-matched, so the pair's dipole moment equals the point model's), each endpoint with its OWN exact emission-time solve and its own receiver-normal booking — the full multipole resummation of the pair (quadrupole, octupole, and true near-field, not a truncation), per unit $p_0$ by the $1/d$ normalization. Validation anchor: $d\to0$ reproduces the dipole rows to $<5\times10^{-4}$ (tested); the frozen-orientation pair rows are static-geometry and dt-exact ($N_t=32/64$ identical). Honesty boundary: at settled-relax orientation the pair endpoints swing with $\hat p$ at arm slew $(d/2)\max|\dot{\hat p}|\approx3.8\,c_f$ — a real assembly's arm is not a valid super-field monopole source at kernel grade, so the relax-pair rows are diagnostic-grade and flagged (the frozen-orientation rows carry the claim).

**The readout.** At $d=p_0\approx0.65a$ the endpoints are not a small perturbation — and the correction goes almost entirely to the caps. At $a=3.4$: the outer radial row amplifies $0.065\to0.210$ (frozen, $3.2\times$) and $0.046\to0.512$ (relax diagnostic, $11\times$); at $a=3.25$: $0.090\to0.338$ frozen, $0.23\to0.79$ relax. The axial-ish saturated orientation puts the pair endpoints at $z\approx\pm1.1$ — cap latitude ($z_O\approx\pm1.23$) — so the near field lands on the layer that was already over-fed. The MIDDLE: the near-field correction is tiny and negative — $\Delta_{\mathrm{nf}}\mathrm{rad}_M=-0.0012$ frozen / $-0.0034$ relax at $3.4$ ($-0.0017/-0.0021$ at $3.25$); the orientational middle hole persists at pair grade ($-0.063$ at $3.4$ vs $-0.060$ dipole). The finite-pair breathing ceiling (phase scan, frozen frame) is $+0.0013$ at $\varepsilon=0.1$ — no better than the dipole-return ceiling. The tangential inner feed survives at pair grade essentially unchanged ($+0.059$ vs $+0.060$) — the durable capability is return-model-robust. All pinned in tests.

**Verdict — fourth scoped negative, and the family closes.** Within the declared response scope — saturable orientation (Sections 47–48 by title), band-limited monopole breathing (Section 49), and now the finite-pair near-field/multipole resummation of both — no cell feeds the equatorial rail layer. The structural signature held through every channel and every return model: *the sea feeds the caps and starves the rail.* The mechanism is now geometrically transparent: everything the saturated-orientation family radiates or holds is axially organized (mean orientations axial-ish, pair endpoints at cap latitude, orientation waves axial-led), and an axially-organized environment cannot push on an equatorial ring. The sea-as-radial-scaffold question is CLOSED at response scope: the sea is the braid's angular-momentum bath (tangential feed, natively demonstrated in Row 4), a cap support, and — at pair grade — an even stronger cap support; it is not the equatorial radial scaffold. Beyond scope (named, not opened): sea self-structure with per-site phase freedom (a standing phonon lattice rather than a driven response) — a formation-history burden, not a response calculation.

**Program consequence.** The radial ledger returns to braid-internal geometry: the item-25 follow-on track (extend the survivability objective with per-layer tangential residuals and dynamically sustained I$\to$M transfer), with the sea retained in all future candidate rows as tangential feed and cap support only — which also relaxes the design problem, since the caps' deficits (the original motivation for sea dressing) are the one thing every sea channel reliably supplies.

Fail-closed: `retainedBranchClaim=false`, `scoreMovement=no_score_increase`; estimate grade (6-site shell with count scaling, declared response family, frozen-frame claim discipline).

Next closure goal: the tangential-closure-targeted geometry search (item 25 follow-on) — re-run the spindle-family configuration search with per-layer tangential residuals in the objective alongside support ratios, seeking dynamically sustained I$\to$M transfer, with the sea booked as tangential feed + cap support at the Section 50 grades; a candidate with all-layer support $\gtrsim0.97$ AND a closing tangential ledger tables Row 5.

## 51. The Tangential-Closure-Targeted Geometry Search: the Inner Brake Is Eliminated in Geometry and the Bare I/M Ledger Closes — the Residual Is One Number (the Cap Credit), and It Must Be Solved as a Fixed Point (2026-07-09)

Executing the Section 50 program consequence (operator-approved): the item-25 follow-on search with the tangential ledger in the objective and the sea booked at Section 50 grades. Owner script extended in place ([spindle-support-ratio-targeted-search.mjs](../../../../scripts/braid-ideal/spindle-support-ratio-targeted-search.mjs): `tangentialLedger`, `searchTangentialClosure`, per-layer support weights, knob freezing; tests 8 passing).

**Declared ledger.** Radial targets: $s_I=s_M=1$ bare (the closed sea family gives them nothing or less); $s_O=1-\mathrm{seaO}$ with the cap credit seaO booked from Section 50 ($0.20$ native-confirmed primary; $0.42$ frozen-pair $\times2$). Tangential: the middle's rail pump belongs to the escapement (excluded, as always); the inner and outer ledgers close iff the layer row is a brake no deeper than the sea's feed cap ($0.12$, natively confirmed and return-model-robust) and not a forward pump (nothing absorbs a forward pump on a sub-field layer). Validation anchor: the single-time booking reproduces v1's rows to the packet digit (support $0.9595/0.8803/0.9796$; $\tau_I=-0.2176$, the known knob-rigid brake; tested).

**Finding 1 — the brake is not knob-rigid; it is objective-rigid.** Under the support-first objective the brake never moved (Section 41); under penalty-dominated tangential objectives it lifts smoothly: $\tau_I$ $-0.22\to-0.13$ (λ=20) $\to-0.05$ (M-weighted cells) with the support sum nearly conserved. The Section 41 "knob-rigid" reading was an artifact of never paying for the brake in the objective.

**Finding 2 — TANGENTIAL-CLOSURE CANDIDATE V2 (named, exported).** At $(q_I,q_O,\alpha_I,\alpha_M,\alpha_O,\theta_I,\theta_O)=(0.481,\,1.036,\,-2.4°,\,-24.4°,\,64.5°,\,-16.7°,\,330.5°)$: bare support $I/M/O=1.003/0.994/0.502$, $\tau_I=+0.0006$, $\tau_O=-0.0005$, closure $0.286$ — the family's best closure on record, co-improving yet again. **The Rows 1–4 first blocker — the $-0.218$ inner tangential brake that set every dispersal clock — is eliminated by geometry alone, with the middle simultaneously at full bare support.** The unlocking knob is the middle tilt ($\alpha_M\approx-24°$): the "rail resists tilting" lore (Section 21) was a closure-metric artifact; under the ledger objective the tilted rail frees the I/M allocation (cadence rises $\times1.10$ via the rail constraint $\omega=c_f/(R_M\cos\alpha_M)$, all layers sub-field). Also confirmed: the middle CAN take full support ($s_M\to1.00$ unconstrained) — the Section 37 sum-rule allocation freedom is real; the binding structure is the joint corridor, not any single layer.

**Finding 3 — the residual is one number, and it is a fixed-point problem.** V2's whole remaining deficit is the bare cap row $s_O=0.50$: the ledger closes iff the sea's cap credit is $\approx0.47$–$0.50$ (`tangentialLedger` closes at seaO $=0.5$, fails at the $0.20$ primary booking; test-pinned). But the claim-grade credit is GEOMETRY-HYPERSENSITIVE: recomputing the Section 50 frozen-pair rows on the found geometries gives credit $0.04$ at V2 (caps at $64.5°$, braid dipole halved), $0.42$ at v1 ($84°$ caps), and $>1.5$ at high-$q_O$/$84°$ variants — while freezing $\alpha_O=84°$ to retain credit stalls the bare ledger entirely ($I/M=0.80/0.87$, $\tau_I=-0.19$). The credit and the bare closure move oppositely through the same knobs ($\alpha_O$, $q_O$). This is the Section 48 geometry-matched-booking lesson recurring at the search level, and it upgrades the standing rule: **no candidate tabling from a constant sea booking — the cap credit must be solved self-consistently with the geometry.** V2 is therefore a bare-ledger champion, credit-gated, NOT tabled.

Fail-closed: `retainedBranchClaim=false`, `scoreMovement=no_score_increase`; single-time rigid evaluation, coordinate-descent grade (no global-optimality claim), sea booked at estimate grades.

Next closure goal: the self-consistent cap-credit fixed-point search — couple the geometry's OWN claim-grade cap credit $\mathrm{credit}(geo,a)$ into the search objective (target $\mathrm{credit}=1-s_O(geo)$ jointly with the bare I/M corridor and the tangential caps, credit computed at frozen-pair grade with a cheap in-loop orientation proxy and verified post-hoc on the Section 50 instrument), over the spindle knobs plus the sea spacing; a jointly-closing self-consistent cell tables Candidate Row 5, and a scoped negative closes the rigid-spindle-plus-response-sea program at declared scope.

## 52. The Self-Consistent Cap-Credit Fixed-Point Search: a Verified Closing Cell Exists — Static-Sea Candidate V3, All Layers in the Corridor With No Dynamic Sea Requirement; Row 5 Tabling Decision to the Operator (2026-07-09)

Executing the Section 51 named follow-on (operator-approved): the cap credit enters the search objective as a per-trial function $\mathrm{credit}(geo,a)$ and the sea spacing joins the knob set. Owner script extended in place ([spindle-support-ratio-targeted-search.mjs](../../../../scripts/braid-ideal/spindle-support-ratio-targeted-search.mjs): `capCreditProxy`, `searchSelfConsistent`; tests 11 passing).

**In-loop credit proxy (declared, anchored, and its trap found).** The proxy books the frozen finite pair (unit monopoles at $\pm p_0(geo)/2$ along the slow-limit orientation — alignment with the cycle-averaged bare causally delayed field, the $\gamma\to0$ environment's own convention) on the outer receivers with the supportRatios booking, $\times2$ FCC-12, at $\approx3.4$ ms/eval. It anchors to the Section 50 instrument to the digit at the Section 50 cells (v1/3.4: $0.419$ vs $0.42$; v1/3.25: $0.676$ vs $0.68$; V2/3.4: $0.033$ vs $0.033$). The trap: at close spacings the coarse orientation average ALIASES on some cells ($N_t=12/24$ gave $0.347$ where $N_t=48$ and the instrument both give $0.238$) — one intermediate "closing cell" was an instrument artifact caught by the dt witness, and a second ($a=2.631$ variant) failed instrument verification ($0.183$ vs proxy $0.279$). Standing rule extended to the sampling level: **per-cell instrument verification with dt witnesses is mandatory before any credit is citable.**

**The verified closing cell — SELF-CONSISTENT STATIC-SEA CANDIDATE V3 (named, exported).** Geometry $(q_I,q_O,\alpha_I,\alpha_M,\alpha_O,\theta_I,\theta_O)=(0.4935,\,1.036,\,-3.65°,\,-29.04°,\,67.5°,\,-12.2°,\,333.5°)$ with the sea shell at $a=2.453$ (6-direction convention, FCC-12 $\times2$ booking, static positions, frozen slow-limit orientations). Bare rows: support $1.0035/1.0008/0.677$, $\tau_I=-0.0032$, $\tau_O=-0.0091$. Cap credit $0.3172$, INSTRUMENT-VERIFIED and dt-stable ($N_t=32/64$ identical on the claim instrument; converged proxy agrees to $<0.01$ — the two orientation conventions coincide at this cell). Sea taxes on I and M at claim grade: $-0.0013/-0.0023$ (negligible). **Dressed rows: $1.0022/0.9985/0.9942$ — all three layers inside the $[0.97,1.03]$ corridor.** Closure $0.2058$ — the family record moves again ($0.324\to0.286\to0.206$ across the search generations; the ledger-first objectives keep finding better closure than the closure-first searches did).

**Why this cell is structurally different from every tabled row before it.** $\tau_I\approx0$ and $\tau_O\approx0$ mean NO sea tangential feed is required, so the sea can be fully STATIC — frozen positions and frozen orientations. That removes, by construction and at seed grade, every environment mechanism that killed Rows 3 and 4: no orientation waves (no M-tax), no relaxation dynamics (no lag collapse, no channel-dependent fast-alignment requirement), no dynamic commensurability band (the credit is a static near-field number; exact causal delays are trivial for static sources). The braid handles its own tangential ledger internally (the Section 51 brake elimination, refined here), the middle is at full bare support, and the sea does the one thing Sections 47–50 proved it reliably does: hold the caps. The middle tilt ($\alpha_M\approx-29°$) remains the load-bearing knob; cadence rises $\times1.14$ through the rail constraint, all layers sub-field (speeds $\approx0.56/1.00/0.45$).

**Caveats for the tabling decision (declared, not hedged away).** (i) Coordinate-descent grade — no global-optimality claim; the cell is a verified point, not a mapped basin (basin polish and a twin-row stability probe belong to the native run's seed block as in Rows 2–4). (ii) The $\times2$ FCC-12 count scaling stands in for true 12-site angular placement; Section 44's cross-check found true placement FAVORABLE (nearly doubles 6-axial margins), but the native build must use true placement and will re-derive the credit. (iii) The slow-limit orientation is the declared $\gamma\to0$ selection; its stability against slow drift is unprobed (a static-orientation environment cannot answer it — the native run's sea back-reaction rows can). (iv) Shell clearance at $a=2.453$: nearest approach of the M ring to a sea-pair endpoint is $\approx1.2$ braid units; static sources keep the root structure trivial, but the near-field steepness at that clearance is the price of the credit.

Fail-closed: `retainedBranchClaim=false`, `scoreMovement=no_score_increase`. The search names and verifies the cell; it authorizes nothing. Tabling Candidate Row 5 is an operator decision.

Next closure goal: operator decision — table Candidate Row 5 (native retained-history release of V3 inside the static frozen-orientation pair-resolved sea at $a=2.453$, true FCC-12 placement, the Section 30 machinery and diagnostics, with the credit re-derived in-build as the seed anchor), or direct further polish (basin mapping / true-placement re-verification on the evaluator stack) first.

## 53. Candidate Row 5 Native Run: Certificate Rejected at the Seed Gate — the Cap Credit Is Polar-Concentrated, True FCC-12 Placement Flips It Negative, and No Release Occurred (2026-07-09)

Executing the tabled Candidate Row 5 (handoff packet block by title, "Candidate Row 5: Static-Sea Release of Self-Consistent Candidate V3"; operator decision (a), 2026-07-09). Owner script extended in place with the tabled `--row=5` selection ([spindle-braid-native-retained-history-confirmation-run.mjs](../../../../scripts/braid-ideal/spindle-braid-native-retained-history-confirmation-run.mjs): `TABLED_ROWS[5]`, `buildStaticPairSeaSites` (true FCC-12 placement, in-build frozen slow-limit orientations, antipodal unit-polarity pair endpoints as static production sources on the native root path), split sea channel preserving the bare-braid $\kappa^*$ fit discipline, in-build credit re-derivation with $N_t$ witnesses, the corridor release gate, and the frozen-sea back-reaction honesty row; tests 16 passing). Verdict: operator **REJECTED** in the run thread, first blocker `corridor_gate_failed_at_in_build_credit`. **No release occurred** — the packet's binding obligation 1 forbids it, so this row's release diagnostics (dispersal clock, corridor-holding, $\tau_I$ without feed, escapement at $\omega\times1.144$, honesty row) were not produced; the run record is the seed-grade gate report (`.tmp/braid-ideal/spindle-braid-native-confirmation/report-row5.json`).

**Seed anchors reproduced to the digit (build validated twice over).** Native seed record at the tabled geometry: $\kappa^*=0.4539$, closure $0.20581$, bare support $1.0036/1.0007/0.6771$ — the Section 52 instrument rows exactly. In-build $p_0=0.8806$ (the packet's $\pm0.436$ endpoints were the rounded $p_0/2$; in-build value $0.4403$). Binding obligation 2 (no aliased sampling): orientation witness $N_t=64/32$ max deviation $0.17°$; credit witness $-0.0338$ vs $-0.0344$; the seed rows are sampling-stable.

**Binding obligation 1 — the decisive finding.** Re-derived in-build at TRUE FCC-12 angular placement, the cap credit is $\mathbf{-0.0338}$ (cycle-averaged $-0.025$; sampling-robust), a correction of $-0.351$ against the tabled instrument value $0.3172$. Dressed support $1.0018/1.0059/\mathbf{0.6434}$ — the cap row is far outside the $[0.97,1.03]$ corridor, and the release gate fired fail-closed at seed grade.

**Root cause, isolated and anchored.** The native booking on the instrument's own 6-axial convention $\times2$ reproduces the tabled credit ($0.3084$ vs $0.3172$ — regularizer-grade agreement, so the build is right and the tabled number is the artifact). Decomposing the 6-axial row: the two sites ON THE BRAID AXIS carry $+0.190$; the four equatorial-axial sites carry $-0.036$. **The cap credit is a polar-site quantity, and the FCC first coordination shell has no polar sites** (all 12 directions at $45°$/equatorial to the axis). The $\times2$ count scaling silently assumed angular uniformity of a polar-concentrated row. This is the Section 44 lesson (exact delays killed the uniform-loop-delay estimate) recurring one level up, and it extends the standing rule again: **no credit booking from count-scaled placement — the in-loop credit function must be computed at true angular placement.** The `capCreditProxy`/`searchSelfConsistent` fixed-point instrument inherits this correction before it can table another cell.

**Recoverability probe (one named orientation, not a search).** The braid-axis-to-lattice orientation was an undeclared convention of the count-scaled instrument. Pointing the braid axis along an FCC nearest-neighbor direction — the most favorable case, placing 2 of the 12 sites at the poles — recovers credit $O=+0.124$ but taxes the middle ($-0.064$): dressed $1.0013/0.9367/0.8010$, still far outside the corridor. The first shell cannot deliver this credit at V3 in any orientation. Named lead for the evaluator track: the FCC SECOND shell (6 axial sites at $a\sqrt2\approx3.47$) has genuine polar coverage; whether its weaker near-field credit (the polar pair alone gave $+0.190$ at $2.453$) plus first-shell rows can close the corridor is an open instrument question — to be answered with true-placement, axis-declared bookings only.

Fail-closed: `retainedBranchClaim=false`, `scoreMovement=no_score_increase`. Durable positives retained from the arc: the bare V3 geometry rows (brake elimination, I/M at full support, closure record $0.2058$) are untouched by this rejection — the blocker is the environment's cap credit, not the braid geometry.

## 54. True-Placement Axis-Declared Credit: No FCC Occupancy Closes — but the Braid's Own Octahedral Cage Does; the Sea's Local Structure Is Braid-Selected, Not Lattice-Imposed (2026-07-09)

Executing the Row 5 run thread's closure goal (Section 53 by title): the fixed-point instrument corrected to true-placement, axis-declared credit — the `capCreditProxy` successor `seaRowsTruePlacement` sums ACTUAL declared sites (no count scaling, ever), books all three layers' sea rows (taxes included), and declares the polar fraction of the cap credit ([spindle-support-ratio-targeted-search.mjs](../../../../scripts/braid-ideal/spindle-support-ratio-targeted-search.mjs), with `searchTruePlacement` over geometry x spacing x shell combination; tests 13 passing).

**Validation anchors.** FCC first shell on V3 at $a=2.453$: O row $-0.029$ (native Section 53 found $-0.034$ — placement-grade agreement), and a NEW finding the count-scaled convention had hidden: FCC1's twelve mid-latitude sites TAX THE MIDDLE ($-0.025$). The axial 6-site shell matched to the old radius reproduces the unscaled 6-direction booking exactly ($0.1586$). All rows dt-exact (static geometry; $N_t=16/32/48$ identical).

**Answer to the Section 53 question: NO FCC occupancy combination closes.** FCC first alone: negative cap credit plus middle tax (native and instrument agree). FCC first plus axial second at $a\sqrt2$: the descent's best totals are $0.944/0.959/0.841$ — the first shell's middle tax and the second shell's distance-weakened axials lose the corridor jointly, at every probed cell. Since a true FCC occupancy cannot fill the second shell while leaving the first vacant, the static-sea FCC family is CLOSED at declared scope: **a braid cannot live at a lattice site of an FCC sea.**

**But the corrected instrument finds a closing cell OUTSIDE FCC — the octahedral cage.** OCTAHEDRAL-CAGE CANDIDATE V4 (named, exported): geometry $(q_I,q_O,\alpha_I,\alpha_M,\alpha_O,\theta_I,\theta_O)=(0.4935,\,1.106,\,+2.85°,\,-30.16°,\,67.5°,\,-4.2°,\,333.5°)$ with SIX octahedral neighbors at site radius $2.326$ (two polar, four equatorial), static, frozen slow-limit orientations. Bare rows $0.9993/1.0061/0.5759$ with $\tau_I=-0.0035$, $\tau_O=-0.0057$; sea rows $+0.0013/-0.0100/+0.4178$ (actual sites, axis-declared: the polar pair carries $111\%$ of the O credit, the equatorial four $-11\%$); **totals $1.0006/0.9961/0.9937$ — all layers in corridor**; bare closure $0.2474$. The Section 52 verification discipline holds: rows dt-witnessed on both stacks' conventions (static sources make them exact), no scaling anywhere.

**Interpretation — the solvation shell made literal.** The Row 5 rejection said the FCC first shell cannot hold the caps; this section says the braid's ledgers close when the first shell is VACANT and six neighbors sit in an octahedral cage with polar coverage. The physical reading: the sea's local structure around a braid is braid-selected, not lattice-imposed — the braid carves its neighborhood like an impurity ejecting nearest neighbors, keeping a coordination cage matched to its own deficit map (polar support need $\to$ polar neighbors). The bulk sea beyond the cage is claim-negligible for the near-field credit. Two structural notes: (i) the stable object this describes is really a braid-plus-cage COMPLEX (a seven-assembly composite), which shades the candidate from "braid in a sea" toward "molecule in a solvent" — vocabulary the corpus should eventually own; (ii) the cage members are themselves assemblies sitting in the braid's near field — their own ledgers (reciprocity) are the honesty row any native run must carry.

**Caveats (declared).** Steric tightness: cage-pair endpoints reach within $\approx0.8$ of the caps, and six sea assemblies of order-unity extent at mutual spacing $\approx3.3$ around a braid of extent $\approx1.1$ must be declared for overlap in-build. Reciprocity unprobed at this grade: the cage members' own support/orientation ledgers in the central braid's near field are not computed here (the frozen-static declaration carries them; the native honesty row reports what they would feel). Coordinate-descent point, not a mapped basin. Closure $0.2474$ sits above V3's $0.2058$ — the corridor was bought with a small closure step back; report drift as finding.

Fail-closed: `retainedBranchClaim=false`, `scoreMovement=no_score_increase`. The instrument names and verifies the cell; tabling Candidate Row 6 is an operator decision.

Next closure goal: operator decision — table Candidate Row 6 (native retained-history release of V4 inside the static octahedral cage at site radius $2.326$, frozen slow-limit orientations, exact-per-element static delays, with the in-build credit re-derivation, the steric declaration, and the cage-reciprocity honesty row as binding obligations), or first run the cage-reciprocity check on the evaluator stack (do the six cage members' own ledgers close well enough to justify the frozen-static declaration?).

## 55. Cage Reciprocity: the Orientation Is Torque-Equilibrated, and the Polar Pair Carries the Credit's Newton-Pair — the Cage Needs a Bulk Anchor or Dynamics; Row 6 Decision to the Operator (2026-07-09)

Executing Section 54's named check (operator route (a)): per-cage-member force and torque rows on the V4 octahedral cage, at kappa*, exact causal delays on the braid legs (softened branch weight — the rail layer's caustic fan crosses external points), static endpoint sums for cage-cage, normalized by the braid's outer-layer centripetal need (`cageReciprocity` on the owner script; tests 14 passing).

**Finding 1 — the frozen orientation is self-consistent.** Net torque on every cage member is zero to instrument precision ($<0.01$ of the corridor scale): the slow-limit alignment is a genuine torque equilibrium of braid field plus cage-cage fields jointly. The frozen-orientation declaration carries no hidden strain.

**Finding 2 — the polar pair carries the credit's reaction.** Net radial force on each polar member: $-0.641$ of the corridor force scale, INWARD, dt-stable ($N_t=24/48$ identical) — the Newton-pair of the $+0.418$ cap credit, as it must be in a delayed-superposition theory at cycle average. The equatorial four carry a small outward residue ($+0.09$–$0.15$, sampling-sensitive at the caustic grade). A static polar member with an unsupported $0.64$-scale inward pull would fall in: **the cage is not self-consistent at cage-only scope.** Its anchor must come from outside the declared system — either the bulk sea beyond (each cage member is itself caged by the next coordination environment, so the regress terminates in a bulk equilibrium condition, the condensed-matter reading), or cage dynamics (co-orbital cage members trading the pull against their own centripetal need — the "molecule rotates" reading). Neither is computed here; both are named.

**What this does and does not do to Row 6.** It does not invalidate the V4 braid rows: the braid-side ledgers (Section 54) are unchanged, and the native machinery holds environment sites static by declaration, so a Row 6 release remains executable with the reciprocity strain DECLARED ($-0.64$ polar, $+0.1$ equatorial, torque-free) and reported in the honesty row. It does bound the claim: a Row 6 survival would certify the braid inside a held cage, not a self-supporting braid-plus-cage complex; the complex-level closure (bulk-boundary equilibrium or co-orbital cage) is a named separate burden — properly the sea lane's own closure question, now with its first quantified number.

Fail-closed: `retainedBranchClaim=false`, `scoreMovement=no_score_increase`.

Next closure goal: operator decision — (a) table Candidate Row 6 (native release of V4 in the held octahedral cage, reciprocity strain declared, braid-side survival as the readout, in-build credit re-derivation and steric declaration binding), or (b) attack the complex-level closure first (bulk-boundary anchor or co-orbital cage) on the evaluator stack before any native run.

The mechanism is the first blocker in its plainest form: **the $+0.418$ cap credit is an attractive near-field with no restoring gradient — the corridor-complete seed is a force balance, not a basin** — and the O layer rides outward into its own credit source ($r_O\ 1.106\to1.41$, support row $0.99\to2.7$) while M slides inward ($1.00\to0.87$, support $0.95\to2.1$). The twin row agrees: a $10^{-3}$ tangential kick separates to $3.7$ by $t=1.03$ — exponential, no return.

**Diagnostic rows (delivered regardless of outcome, packet order).** (1) Dispersal clock: above. (2) Support corridor along the release: collapses immediately and anisotropically (I alone holds $\sim1.00\to0.90$) — allocation runs to the caps, the Row 4 lesson at static grade. (3) Tangential rows: $\tau_I\approx0$ at the seed and NO brake spiral develops — **the Section 51 geometry-eliminated inner brake confirms natively at V4** (first native test; durable positive). But the tangential ledger then goes uniformly positive ($\tau_I\to+0.045$, $\tau_O\to+0.037$): the cage near-field is a net forward pump, and $\beta_M$ leaves the rail UPWARD, monotone ($1.00\to1.56$, zero rail crossings) — **the program's first upward rail loss**; the speed-attractor pin does not recapture from above once the axis tips. (4) $h_{\mathrm{act}}$ click ledger at $\omega\approx1.156$: 4 transitions, all integer $+1$ — the M pair's same-source roots open at release (the poised clicker at the raised cadence, Rows 1–4 continuity) and the O pair's open at the capture event; no chatter. (5) Cage honesty row: polar strain $\mathbf{-0.641}$ FLAT along the whole release (no growth, no sign change — the held-cage declaration carries exactly the declared Section 55 strain), torques $\le0.001$ throughout; equatorial residue small and sampling-sensitive ($0.03$–$0.18$ at the caustic grade, as declared). (6) **Axis-wobble watch — the run's genuine finding: the released braid nutates immediately.** The best-fit rigid-rotation axis tilts $1.1°$ by $t=0.1$, $9.5°$ by $t=1.0$ ($35°$ by $t=1.6$ at the $dt/2$ witness), azimuth drifting — Section 55's reciprocity made the cage members torque-free about their own centers, but the braid receives a net torque from the polar-pair offset fields. The prescribed family is precession-free by construction, so this is the first native nutation number for the spin-mapping question, and it is also the coupling that degrades the rail (the pin is defined transverse to an axis that is tipping).

**Interpretation.** The static-environment family has now failed in its strongest form: a corridor-complete, torque-equilibrated, polar-covering cage — every seed ledger inside the corridor simultaneously — disperses an order of magnitude FASTER than the bare braid ($0.093$ vs $0.83$ rotations). The credit that closes the seed ledger is the very attraction that captures the cap: at static grade, corridor closure and dynamic confinement are not merely different questions — the first actively purchases the second's failure mode. Together with the Row 5 polar-credit rejection this closes the held-static environment scope on the braid side as well as the cage side (Section 55 already showed the cage needs an anchor); what remains for the sea lane is DYNAMICS — the co-orbital cage (members trading the $-0.641$ pull against their own centripetal need, with the phase ripple and the nutation torque as the first coupled channels) or the bulk-boundary equilibrium. The claim boundary was honored and returns strengthened: even the held cage is not survivable at V4.

Fail-closed: `retainedBranchClaim=false`, `scoreMovement=no_score_increase`. ## 57. The Seed-Grade Radial Stability Matrix: Basins Are Now Computable — the Held Cage Fails It Exactly as the Native Run Did, the Bare Braid's Shape Is Restoring, and the Size Mode Points at d0 (2026-07-09)

Executing the Section 56 program consequence (operator route (a)): the non-symmetric Jacobian K = d(net radial force)/d(displacement) over the slow coordinates (r_I, r_M, r_O, a_cage) at frozen rotation and frozen kappa* — the instrument that converts "corridor found" into "basin or not, with directions" (`radialStabilityMatrix` on the owner script, field-of-values readout via the symmetric part; tests 15 passing). The system is non-conservative, so K is not a curvature of any energy; a negative-definite symmetric part is the sufficient restoring condition.

**Validation — the instrument reproduces the native Row 6 mechanism at seed grade, in seconds.** Dressed V4: symmetric-part spectrum (+1.09, -0.18, -2.07, -4.02) — NOT a basin — with the escape eigenvector (+0.005, +0.145, -0.459, +0.877): the cap-out/cage mode, exactly the native capture. The cage member's own diagonal is anti-restoring (+0.77): the -0.641 pull steepens along its coordinate. This gate would have rejected Rows 5 and 6 before any native run; it is now the standing pre-tabling requirement.

**Finding — the bare braid's SHAPE is a basin; its SIZE is not, and that is physics.** Bare V4 radial block: spectrum (-0.50, -2.31, -4.03), all restoring — the first basin in the program's history. The bare braid is not balanced (cap net force -0.24 of scale, inward), so it relaxes; Newton relaxation converges in SHAPE (q_I -> 0.443, q_O -> 0.826 at the V4 tilts) but stalls with a uniform ~0.13-scale inward residual on all layers: the DILATION mode. In the fitted-kappa* convention the sum rule caps mean support below 1 (the relaxed state sits at 0.899/0.885/0.896), so the bare braid's quasi-static fate is slow uniform CONTRACTION — not dispersal (the restoring shape block prevents shape loss). Contraction arrests where near-field discreteness bites: the declared coincidence stratum d0 = R_MCB, the collapse-arresting radius (operator d0 decision, 2026-07-08). Three program threads converge here unprompted: the MCB-via-spindle capture, the discreteness-as-confinement hypothesis (Section 35 weak form), and the d0 ontology decision — the radial ledger's missing ~10% is not the sea's job and not geometry's job; it is the d0 floor's job.

**Scope and gaps (declared).** Quasi-static (no delay-memory modes); no tilt/nutation coordinate yet (the Row 6 nutation is outside this block — named gap); the dilation analysis mixes the fitted-kappa* gauge with the frozen-kappa displacement convention — the honest size-mode treatment needs the kappa-consistent absolute scale (kappa, epsilon, c_f -> R*) in the quasi-static frame for the first time.

Fail-closed: `retainedBranchClaim=false`, `scoreMovement=no_score_increase`.

## 58. The Absolute-Scale Instrument: the Speed Pin Is Also the Size Pin — the Bare Braid Self-Equilibrates With a Basin at a Derived kappa-Scale Size; Self-Equilibrated Candidate V5 (2026-07-09)

Executing the Section 57 operator ruling (route (a)). Two corrections built the frame: (i) the quasi-static displacements must run at the RAIL-PINNED cadence — during contraction the middle stays on the field-speed rail (the natively confirmed attractor), so omega = c_f/(R_M cos aM) responds to R_M; under that pin wake forces scale 1/lambda^2 against needs at 1/lambda, support gains 1/lambda, and the size mode acquires a restoring feedback — the speed pin IS the size pin; (ii) kappa must be FROZEN once — the per-configuration kappa refit is a gauge that exactly absorbs the dilation gain (the mechanical content of the fitted-kappa sum rule), which is why the size mode looked unbalanced in every earlier frame (`railPinnedEquilibrium` on the owner script, absolute-displacement Newton with kapFixed; tests 16 passing).

**Result 1 — the bare braid has a true radial equilibrium with a basin.** From V4's shape: residuals ~1e-6, rail-pinned spectrum fully restoring; the joint solution relaxes shape AND size together (the naive uniform-scaling estimate does not govern alone). The equilibrium is not imposed by any corridor — support = 1 on every layer BY DEFINITION at F = 0; the corridor hunt of the entire arc was the search for this fixed point through a gauge that hid it.

**Result 2 — the joint fixed point with the tangential ledger exists: SELF-EQUILIBRATED CANDIDATE V5 (named, exported).** Alternating angle-descent (tau_I, tau_O -> 0) with radial re-equilibration converges in three passes: geometry $(q_I,q_O,\alpha_I,\alpha_M,\alpha_O,\theta_I,\theta_O)=(0.55,\,0.75,\,-27.15°,\,+16.24°,\,64.5°,\,-16.2°,\,339.5°)$; radial residual $\sim4\times10^{-6}$ with basin $(-0.63,\,-2.00,\,-6.27)$; $\tau_I=0.0006$, $\tau_O=0.0004$; the middle's $+0.227$ rail pump is the escapement's, as always. NO environment: the sea program's conclusion stands (bath and cap support are its jobs), but this object needs neither for its radial ledger — the caps sit at support 1 by self-adjustment of shape and size.

**Result 3 — the braid's absolute size is a derived constant.** At the fixed point $R_M^{\mathrm{eq}} = \lambda/\kappa^{\mathrm{frozen}} \approx 3.49$ in units $\kappa\varepsilon^2/c_f^2$ — the same scale family as the declared $d_0=R_{\mathrm{MCB}}$ floor, with the ratio a pure number (modulo the open MCB constant): the equilibrium sits well ABOVE the floor, so the arrest is kappa-scale self-support, not discreteness rescue. The braid size joins the family's derived quantities; the mass-map lane should note the anchor.

**Scope and gaps (declared).** Quasi-static seed grade: no tilt/nutation coordinate (Row 6's native killer — the axis modes are NOT in this matrix; a Row 7 tabling must carry the axis-wobble watch and should expect it to be decisive), no delay-memory modes, single-time rigid booking on the braid legs, angle-descent at refit-kappa gauge (tau zeros are gauge-robust). Closure residual at V5 is 0.425 — the closure metric and the ledger metrics now disagree openly; the arc's evidence (Rows 1-6) says the ledgers govern survival.

Fail-closed: `retainedBranchClaim=false`, `scoreMovement=no_score_increase`. The instrument names and verifies the fixed point; tabling Candidate Row 7 is an operator decision.

## 59. The Tilt Block: Global Tilt Is an Exact Null, Relative Tilts Are Restoring at V5 — the Stability Gate Now Covers the Row 6 Killer; Row 7 Decision to the Operator (2026-07-09)

Executing the Section 58 operator ruling (route (a)): the stability gate extended with the tilt coordinates — per-layer plane inclinations $\eta_L$ about a transverse axis, generalized force = cycle-averaged transverse torque per layer at kappa* on exact causal roots (`tiltStiffness` on the owner script; tests 17 passing).

**Validation row built into the physics.** For a bare braid, a GLOBAL tilt is a symmetry (isotropy), so $K_{\mathrm{tilt}}\cdot(1,1,1)$ must vanish — and it does, to numerical zero. Two instrument notes with physical content: (i) the row sums vanish but the COLUMN sums do not — in a causal-delay theory internal torques need not cancel instantaneously (the field in flight carries angular momentum), so the asymmetry of $K$ is physics, not error; (ii) that same asymmetry pollutes the symmetric-part bound with the global mode, so the claim-bearing readout is the exact quotient spectrum (deflate the null, take the $2\times2$ eigenvalues — complex values would be whirl).

**Verdict at V5: restoring.** Relative-tilt eigenvalues $-0.1247$ and $-0.7485$ — both real, both negative, dt-exact ($N_t=8/16$ identical). The strongest single coupling is inner-tilt$\to$middle-torque ($+0.90$), consistent with the Row 6 native phenomenology (the nutation grew through the middle once the inner tipped), and at V5 the net response is restoring anyway. DECLARED CAVEAT, standing: the layers carry spin angular momentum, so the true linear dynamics is gyroscopic-circulatory ($\lambda^2M+\lambda G+K$ with asymmetric $K$); restoring $K$ is the seed-grade gate, and flutter-class instabilities remain the native run's question.

**Gate status.** The pre-tabling stability gate now comprises: rail-pinned radial block (basin, Section 58), tangential ledger ($\tau_I,\tau_O\approx0$), and the tilt block (global null + restoring relative modes) — every mode class that has killed a native run is covered at seed grade. V5 passes all three. No prior candidate would have.

Fail-closed: `retainedBranchClaim=false`, `scoreMovement=no_score_increase`.

## 61. The Gyroscopic-Circulatory Axis Analysis: Seed-Grade Flutter at V5, Independent of the Rail Pump — the Section 60 Route-(b) Question Answered, and the Pre-Tabling Gate Gains Its Axis-Dynamics Row (2026-07-10)

Executing the Section 60 route (b) (operator thread; co-owned with braid-angular-momentum-spin queue item 12, deliverable 4, per its [packet](../braid-angular-momentum-spin/spindle-spin-scaffold-rederivation-packet.md)). Owner script extended in place ([spindle-support-ratio-targeted-search.mjs](../../../../scripts/braid-ideal/spindle-support-ratio-targeted-search.mjs), `gyroscopicTiltAnalysis`; tests 19 passing): the true linearized axis dynamics at SELF_EQUILIBRATED_V5 over both transverse tilt directions per layer, $q=(\eta^x_L,\eta^y_L)$, with $M=\mathrm{diag}(m_L)$ from the layer kinematics ($m_L=\rho_L^2+2z_L^2$, cycle-averaged, unit site weight), $G$ from the layer spin angular momenta ($J_L=2\rho_L^2\omega$: $m\ddot\eta_x+J\dot\eta_y=T_x$, $m\ddot\eta_y-J\dot\eta_x=T_y$), and the full $6\times6$ measured tilt Jacobian $K$ (both torque components under both tilt axes, same cycle-averaged $\kappa^*$-scaled exact-causal-root evaluator as `tiltStiffness`).

**Correction to the declared pencil, forced by the physics.** $\lambda^2M+\lambda G+K$ is not the honest linearization: the layers carry nonzero baseline axial torques (the middle's rail pump, $\tau_M=+0.423$ in torque units; $\tau_I,\tau_O\approx0$), and the spin transport $d(J\hat{\mathbf n})/dT$ contributes $\dot J\,\hat{\mathbf n}$ terms. The pencil is $\lambda^2M+\lambda G+\Gamma-K$ with $\Gamma$ the antisymmetric block of the baseline torques. The instrument found this through its own validation row: the cross-block row sums of the measured $K$ equal the baseline layer torques exactly (witnessed to $7\times10^{-4}$ against scale $0.9$) — **a global tilt reorients the pump torque; the rail pump enters the axis sector** — and only $K-\Gamma$ annihilates the global tilts, restoring the exact double zero root that the quotient discipline deflates.

**Validation rows (all passing).** Baseline transverse torques $\sim10^{-16}$ (tilt equilibrium automatic); cycle-averaged response exactly $z$-rotation covariant ($E=A$, $D=-B$ to $\sim10^{-15}$); the $x$-$x$ block reproduces the Section 59 tilt block to $10^{-9}$ with its global null at $10^{-15}$; Durand-Kerner residual $\sim10^{-16}$ on the degree-12 determinant; exact global pair at numerical zero, deflated.

**Verdict: FLUTTER, dt-exact ($N_t=8/16$ identical to $10^{-4}$; $\eta$-step halved, $3\times10^{-4}$).** The quotient spectrum carries a growing whirl pair $\lambda=+0.183\pm0.382\,i$ — e-fold $\approx5.5$ absolute-time units ($\approx0.9$ rotation), whirl period $\approx2.7$ rotations — with mode shape $(I,M,O)$ amplitudes $(0.62,1.00,0.26)$: middle-dominated, inner strongly participating, caps near-spectator — the Section 59 inner-tilt$\to$middle-torque coupling ($+0.90$) is the flutter pathway, now with its sign verdict reversed by the gyroscopic-circulatory terms (restoring quasi-static $K$, growing dynamics). A second weak fast pair sits at $+0.037\pm1.406\,i$. **The Section 60 route-(b) question is answered at seed grade: the axis sector is independently fatal.** The pump-absorbed counterfactual ($\tau_M$ removed from $\Gamma$; $K$ kept as measured) still flutters, slightly faster ($+0.275\pm0.435\,i$): absorbing the rail pump does not stabilize the axis. Native corroboration: mode class and direction match the Row 7 nutation growth ($2.4°\to7.8°$ over $t=0.2\to1.0$, preceding shape loss); the frozen-geometry linear rate underpredicts the native mid-window rate by a factor of a few (the released braid was simultaneously expanding — a coupling outside this pencil).

**Consequences for the lane.** (i) The pre-tabling stability gate is incomplete as constituted: the quasi-static tilt block (Section 59) passes a configuration whose true axis dynamics grows. The gate's axis row should be the gyroscopic-circulatory quotient spectrum (computable in seconds on the owner instrument); no successor candidate row should table on the static tilt block alone. (ii) The Section 60 route-(a) $d_0$-from-survival program inherits a second obligation: a marginal-stratum re-release that absorbs the pump still faces the axis instability, so survival requires axis-sector absorption — a tilt-sector analog of the escapement — or a mode-class change from the environment/dynamics that supplies it. (iii) Instrument caveats, declared: cycle-averaged rigid-layer reduction; kinematic spin transport only (no delay-memory tilt-rate damping block — the causal wake's velocity response is not in $G$); frozen geometry; the counterfactual cell is heuristic (its $K$ retains the measured pump content while $\Gamma$ drops it, and its global pair is deflated by identification rather than exactly). The corpus statement of the scaffold and this analysis lives in [angular-momentum-and-spin.md](../../../../content/markdown/aaa/philosophy-history/theory-bridges/angular-momentum-and-spin.md#spindle-braid-spin-scaffold).

Fail-closed: `retainedBranchClaim=false`, `scoreMovement=no_score_increase`; seed-grade instrument on prescribed worldlines; nothing here authorizes a release or re-release.

Next closure goal: operator decision — (a) the $d_0$-from-survival instrument (Section 60 route (a)) now paired with the axis obligation: locate the marginal brake-equals-pump stratum AND name the axis-sector absorber the re-release will carry, with the gyroscopic-circulatory quotient spectrum as a pre-tabling gate row; or (b) the axis-sector absorber study first — what dynamics (delay-memory damping, environment coupling, escapement clicking under tilt) can turn the $+0.18$ growth pair restoring at seed grade.

## 63. The Delay-Memory Tilt-Rate Block: the Wake's Velocity Response Is ANTI-DAMPING in the Axis Sector — the Completed Pencil Flutters Harder, and the Absorber Requirement Is Order-One (2026-07-10)

Executing the Section 62 route (b) — the Section 61 axis-sector absorber study, first candidate (delay-memory damping), which doubled as closing the Section 61 declared caveat (operator thread, concurrent with the Row 8 thread; the two results compose, below). Owner script extended in place ([spindle-support-ratio-targeted-search.mjs](../../../../scripts/braid-ideal/spindle-support-ratio-targeted-search.mjs), `gyroscopicTiltAnalysisFull`; tests 21 passing on this owner suite). Measurement: for each readout sample the worldline family carries zero layer tilt and a constant tilt rate at the readout time (the causal roots then see the *past* tilt — the delay-memory content — plus the intrinsic velocity response), and the cycle-averaged $\kappa^*$-scaled torques on exact causal roots are differenced centrally in the rate: the $6\times6$ block $D=\partial T/\partial\dot\eta$, both torque components under both tilt axes. Completed pencil: $\lambda^2M+\lambda(G-D)+\Gamma-K$, with $P(0)=\Gamma-K$ unchanged, so the exact global-tilt double zero and its deflation carry over.

**Validation rows.** Rate blocks exactly $z$-rotation covariant ($\sim10^{-15}$); static rows reproduce Section 61 ($A$-block global null, pump witness); global pair at numerical zero, deflated; Durand-Kerner residual $\sim10^{-16}$; witnesses — $N_t=8/16$ identical, rate step halved identical to $10^{-4}$.

**Result 1 — the wake's tilt-rate response is anti-damping.** The diagonal of the measured block is positive on every layer ($\partial T_x/\partial\dot\eta_x=+0.096/+0.101/+0.089$ for I/M/O): the delayed wake torques *along* the tilt rate — the axis-sector analog of the tangential anti-damping pump, now measured in the tilt sector. The largest single entry is again the inner$\to$middle pathway ($\partial T^M_x/\partial\dot\eta^I_x=-0.524$). The rate row sums are nonzero (a global tilt-rate is not a symmetry; the imbalance posts to the wake ledger) — reported as physics rows.

**Result 2 — the completed pencil flutters harder; the Section 61 verdict stands and strengthens.** Quotient spectrum with the measured block: THREE growing whirl pairs, max $\lambda=+0.199\pm2.412\,i$ (a fast pair near the free-precession band now grows fastest), with the slow pair at $+0.196\pm0.277\,i$ and a third at $+0.140\pm1.157\,i$; the growth is monotone in the block scale ($0\to0.5\to1$: $+0.183\to+0.194\to+0.199$). Hoped-for stabilization from delay-memory damping is REJECTED: there is no damping there, only more pump.

**Result 3 — the absorber requirement is order-one.** The isotropic diagonal damping needed to turn the full quotient spectrum restoring is $d^*\approx1.02$ ($\kappa^*$-scaled torque per unit tilt rate) — about half the middle layer's spin angular momentum $J_M=1.92$ per unit rate, and an order of magnitude above every entry of the measured wake block. The axis sector needs a genuine absorber, not a trim.

**Composition with the Row 8 result (Section 62).** The two concurrent negatives converge on one instrument. The remaining bare-braid absorber candidate is the escapement clicking under tilt — the middle's absorptive click channel is not in this cycle-averaged partner-channel evaluator — and sizing its tilt-sector projection against $d^*\approx1$ requires exactly the dt-converged, impulse-resolved click booking that Section 62 names as its own follow-up: at the current chart booking the click impulse is nowhere regulator-converged, so an escapement-under-tilt number booked today would inherit the same $22$–$114\%$ dt swings. The dt-converged escapement booking is therefore the shared prerequisite of both forks; behind it stands the sea's angular-momentum-bath coupling as the non-bare absorber candidate, now with a quantitative target ($d^*$) to meet.

Fail-closed: `retainedBranchClaim=false`, `scoreMovement=no_score_increase`; seed-grade instrument on prescribed worldlines; nothing here authorizes a release or re-release.

Next closure goal: the dt-converged impulse-resolved escapement booking (the Section 62 route (a), now doubly motivated): resolve the per-click impulse to regulator independence, then (i) re-map the brake-equals-pump stratum and (ii) project the click impulse onto the tilt sector against the $d^*\approx1.02$ absorber requirement — a joint answer decides whether the bare braid can supply its own axis absorber or the burden passes to the sea bath.
