## 64. The Dt-Converged Click Booking Exists and Is Cheap; the Escapement Covers Its Own Tilt Diagonal Inside the Measured Click Band — but No Middle-Only Absorber Closes the Axis Sector at Any Strength; the Sea's Quantitative Target Is Named (2026-07-10)

Executing the Sections 62–63 shared prerequisite (operator thread). Owner script extended in place ([self-hit-brake-central-measurement.mjs](../../../../scripts/braid-ideal/self-hit-brake-central-measurement.mjs), blocks (D) `impulseResolvedClickImpulse` / `stepWindowBookingError` and (E) `escapementTiltProjection`; tests 8 passing) plus the per-layer absorber option on the gyroscopic instrument ([spindle-support-ratio-targeted-search.mjs](../../../../scripts/braid-ideal/spindle-support-ratio-targeted-search.mjs) `extraDampingLayers`; tests 22 passing). All rows on the production runtime read-only, prescribed controlled crossing (the vt095 accelerating hinge), same integrand form as the accepted stratum-sweep machinery.

**Result 1 — the dt-converged booking exists and is cheap.** Anchoring the integration window on the crossing time itself (no outer step exists in the booking) and integrating adaptively on a log-spaced grid converges the signed per-click impulse to $<10^{-3}$ relative in a few hundred production solves ($\sim1$ s): $\rho_c=0.01$ absorbed fraction $2.85$ (the accepted fixed-grid $2.6884$ class; the difference is the declared wider window and near-onset resolution), $\rho_c=0.05$: $0.47$. The step-laid window's fragility mechanism reproduces on the controlled crossing: booked-in-step impulse scatters by MORE than its own mean as the crossing phase moves within the step (spread/mean $>2.7$ at $\Delta t=0.0025$), because the impulse density is log-concentrated at onset where linear substeps laid from step boundaries cannot resolve it. The converged booking is the named replacement: crossing-time root-finding plus log-spaced adaptive integration, adoptable by the native run per click event.

**Result 2 — the escapement covers its own tilt diagonal inside the measured click band.** The converged impulse is nearly depth-independent (the $\log\rho_c$ divergence dominates), with brake sensitivity $S=\partial I/\partial v_{\mathrm{exc}}<0$ measured across crossing-acceleration cells; projecting a middle-layer tilt rate through the click geometry ($\delta v=-\dot\eta\,z_M\cos\phi$, same sign on both antipodal members; click torque $-z_M I\cos\phi$; phase-uniform clicking) gives the click channel's middle-diagonal damping $d_{\mathrm{click}}=\nu|S|z_M^2/2$: $\approx0.29$ at the kinematic click bound ($18$/rot) and $\approx3.2$ at the Row 8 measured band ($200$/rot), crossing the isotropic requirement $d^*\approx1.02$ at $\approx63$ clicks/rot — inside the measured $164$–$216$ band. Estimate grade, declared: unit bridge from the controlled crossing to V5 evaluator units by the coupling ratio (radius mismatch $O(1)$), the entry-excess proxy, phase-uniform clicking.

**Result 3 — the structural negative that reshapes the absorber question: no middle-only damping closes the axis sector, at ANY strength.** On the completed pencil, middle-only damping saturates at a growth floor $\approx+0.12$ even at $d_M=8$; inner-only saturates at $\approx+0.077$; inner+middle at $4$ each still leaves $+0.059$ (a slow cap-participating pair survives). The flutter is distributed across all three layers, and the two sub-field layers (inner, caps) have no click channel of their own — **the bare braid cannot supply its own axis absorber through the escapement alone**. Declared gap: the click response to *inner* tilt rate through the delayed wake (the $-0.524$ pathway) would appear as off-diagonal click damping and is not booked here — the one remaining bare-braid route, named.

**Result 4 — the sea's quantitative target.** With the middle's click damping at the measured band ($d_M=3.2$), the axis sector closes at inner+cap damping $d_I=d_O\approx0.48$; at the kinematic click bound ($d_M=0.29$) the requirement is $\approx2.9$ — the chattering escapement buys the environment a factor $\sim6$. The convergence with the sea program is exact in structure: the natively demonstrated sea channels are the cap support and the angular-momentum bath — axially organized, feeding exactly the layers whose damping is otherwise unreachable — and they now carry a number to meet: $d_{I,O}^{\mathrm{sea}}\approx0.5$ in the $\kappa^*$-scaled torque-per-tilt-rate units, at the measured click band.

Fail-closed: `retainedBranchClaim=false`, `scoreMovement=no_score_increase`; controlled-crossing and seed-grade pencil instruments on prescribed worldlines; nothing here authorizes a release or re-release.

Next closure goal: operator decision — (a) the off-diagonal click-response booking (does the middle's click channel damp *inner* tilt rate through the delayed wake pathway — the last bare-braid absorber route; a yes at the $0.5$-class scale would re-open a bare candidate row, a no closes the bare axis sector for good); or (b) the sea-lane absorber feasibility estimate: can a phase-matched responsive sea supply $d_{I,O}\approx0.5$ tilt-rate damping on the inner and cap layers at the confining-band spacing, with exact per-pair delays (the Sections 44–47 discipline); or (c) adopt the converged booking in the native-run thread and re-map the Row 8 stratum with it.

## 65. The Off-Diagonal Click Route Closes: the Phase-Locked Pump Modulation Is Measured at the 0.3-Class, the Coupling It Buys Is an Order Below Requirement and Cannot Move the Flutter — the Bare Braid Cannot Absorb Its Own Axis Sector (2026-07-10)

Executing the Section 64 route (a) (operator thread). Owner script extended in place ([spindle-support-ratio-targeted-search.mjs](../../../../scripts/braid-ideal/spindle-support-ratio-targeted-search.mjs), `clickOffDiagonalEstimate` + `velocityBlockAdd` on the completed pencil; tests 23 passing). The chain under test: layer-$L$ tilt rate $\to$ delayed-wake modulation of the middle's tangential (pump) force resolved in the middle's rotation phase $\to$ modulated click depth $\to$ click torque $-z_MI\cos\phi$ correlated with the modulation. Only the first-harmonic (phase-locked) component of the pump response survives the phase average, so the claim-bearing measurement is the Fourier $\cos\phi/\sin\phi$ projection of $\partial F^M_{\mathrm{tan}}(\phi)/\partial\dot\eta_L$ on exact causal roots.

**Result 1 — the modulation is real and measured** ($N_\phi=16/32$ identical; DC components vanish to $10^{-5}$, a built-in witness — the cycle-averaged pump does not respond to tilt rate at linear order, only its phase structure does). First-harmonic magnitudes: inner $0.289$, outer $0.201$, middle-self $0.091$. The inner$\to$middle pathway leads again.

**Result 2 — the coupling it buys is an order below requirement.** Through the declared chain (Section 64 sensitivity $|S|$ in V5 units, accumulation-time$\times$click-rate $\sim1$, phase-uniform clicking): $D_{\mathrm{offdiag}}(M\!\leftarrow\!I)\approx0.10$, $(M\!\leftarrow\!O)\approx0.07$, $(M\!\leftarrow\!M)\approx0.03$ — against the $\approx0.5$-class inner/cap damping the pencil requires.

**Result 3 — inserted into the pencil, the coupling cannot move the verdict.** At the estimated magnitude, both structures (direct and quadrature), both signs, and with $3\times$ headroom: best cell improves the max growth by $0.008$ ($+0.199\to+0.191$); most cells worsen it. The off-diagonal click route is impotent against the axis-sector growth.

**Verdict — the bare-braid axis sector closes at estimate grade.** Combining Sections 61–65: the quasi-static tilt block is restoring but the gyroscopic-circulatory dynamics grows (§61); the wake's own tilt-rate response is anti-damping (§63); middle-diagonal click damping is achievable at the measured click band but cannot close the sector at any strength (§64); and the off-diagonal click coupling is an order too small and pencil-impotent (§65). Every axis-absorber channel the bare braid owns is now measured or estimate-bounded, and none closes the sector. **The axis-sector absorber burden passes to the environment**, with the named quantitative target: $d_{I,O}\approx0.48$ tilt-rate damping on the inner and cap layers at the measured click band ($\approx2.9$ at the kinematic bound). The sea's natively demonstrated channels — cap support and the angular-momentum bath, axially organized — feed exactly these layers; whether a phase-matched responsive sea reaches the $0.5$-class at the confining-band spacing with exact per-pair delays is now the sea lane's sharpest open number. Caveat rows, declared: the chain estimate carries the $\tau\nu\sim1$ and phase-uniform-clicking assumptions and the controlled-crossing unit bridge; a native booking could move the coupling by factors, but the shortfall is an order plus pencil impotence across signs — the scoped negative is robust to estimate-grade error.

Fail-closed: `retainedBranchClaim=false`, `scoreMovement=no_score_increase`; estimate-grade chain on prescribed worldlines; nothing here authorizes a release, and the bare-sector closure is a scoped negative at its stated grade, not a theorem.

Next closure goal: the sea-lane axis-absorber feasibility estimate (Section 64 route (b), now the only open absorber route): can a phase-matched responsive sea supply $d_{I,O}\approx0.5$ tilt-rate damping on the inner and cap layers at the confining-band spacing ($R\approx3.25$–$3.5$), with exact per-pair causal delays and the reorientation-lag discipline of Sections 44–47 — and does the same configuration keep the cap radial support and tangential feed it already owns.

## 66. The Native Impulse-Resolved Booking (Section 64 Route (c) Executed): the Row 7/8 Click Train Was a Quadrature Artifact — the Converged Native Brake Is CONTINUOUS and Caps at 2/3 of the Pump (No Marginal Stratum Exists Anywhere in $(0, 0.112]$), and the Native Escapement-Under-Tilt Response Is a Rate-Sign-Following PUMP; the Section 64 Middle-Diagonal Damping Row Is Superseded at Native Grade (2026-07-10)

Executing Section 64 route (c) in the native-run operator thread, concurrent with the Sections 64–65 controlled-crossing thread (reconciliation below). Owner script extended in place ([spindle-braid-native-retained-history-confirmation-run.mjs](../../../../scripts/braid-ideal/spindle-braid-native-retained-history-confirmation-run.mjs): `chartWindowIntegrate` REPLACED by the impulse-resolved integrator + `--impulse-map` and `--tilt-projection` instruments; tests 29 passing). Solver-ownership discipline held: every integrand sample remains a production `solveDirectedRelation` root row; no parallel booking path.

**The native instrument.** The per-step 32-substep uniform trapezoid is replaced by the window ODE integrator the booking actually is: $\dot{(\delta v)} = F(\tau, \delta v)$ with the fold factor $1/D_s \sim 1/(\beta-1)$ feeding the booked impulse back into the integrand near the rail (stiff), integrable $|\tau-\tau^*|^{-1/2}$ fold singularities, and root-count jump discontinuities (click events). Scheme: adaptive embedded Euler/trapezoid pair with per-step error control on the booked impulse (declared `atol`/`rtol`), event localization by step rejection to a declared floor, and a **tangency guard** — inside $|D_s| < g$ ($g = 2\times10^{-3}$, declared, $\ll$ the canonical soft $0.02$) the pointwise rows are noise-dominated (the reported $D_s$ of a near-tangent root carries the root-position uncertainty, so $1/D_s$ is unbounded numerical noise) and the booking reads the guard-regularized $m = D_T D_s/(D_s^2+g^2)$; outside it, the unsoftened density-of-states $m = D_T/D_s$ per the chart contract. The guard is a declared regulator with its own halving witness: measured per cell, never assumed away.

**Instrument validation.** (i) Refined uniform-ladder reference (the old scheme continued to $n = 16{,}384$ substeps — an ODE-consistent limit): agreement $\le0.4\%$ on every native window class (smooth, near-rail persistent-root, violent brake) at $\sim100\times$ fewer solves. (ii) The release-instant poised window: the refined ladder converges to $\sim0$ and the guarded booking books $\sim10^{-16}$, where unguarded event-resolved sampling diverges to $\pm10^4$ scan-dependent noise — the same near-onset resolution disease the Section 64 controlled-crossing instrument measured as booked-in-step spread/mean $>2.7$, now diagnosed at its root (solver-tolerance tangency slivers) and excluded by construction. (iii) dt-halving on the absorbed fraction: $1.8$–$2.1\%$ on every cell (Row 8 booking: $22$–$114\%$). (iv) Cell-level guard halving at $\rho_c = 0.0504$: $3.9\%$. The native absorbed-fraction rows are regulator-converged, first in the lane.

**Result 1 — the Row 7/8 escapement phenomenology was a quadrature artifact.** At the converged native booking there is NO click train and NO hunting at any stratum: zero rail crossings on every cell (Row 7 booked 12 crossings / 91 clicks at $\rho_c = 0.05$; Row 8 booked $164$–$216$ clicks/rotation); the only click events are the two $t=0$ poised-clicker boundary openings. The $2$–$24\times$ over-absorption (Section 62), the two-sided hunting, and the $\rho_c = 0.01$ destructive coincidence push were under-resolved-trapezoid artifacts of the near-tangency zone. The converged same-source channel is a **continuous partial brake**, not a discrete escapement.

**Result 2 — the converged brake caps at $\approx 2/3$ pump; no marginal stratum exists natively.** The `--impulse-map` re-map (Row 8 grid extended to $[0.005, 0.112]$, $0.25$-rotation holds, seed/gate protocol identical to Rows 7/8 — gate PASSED whole, $\kappa_{\mathrm{eq}} = 0.28623$ reproduced): absorbed pump fraction $0.667/0.664/0.660/0.653/0.639/0.613/0.562/0.532/0.494$ ascending the nine strata — monotone decreasing, everywhere BELOW $1$, dt-clean throughout. $\beta_M$ climbs off the rail on every cell ($1.135$ at $0.25$ rot vs $1.128$ soft-reference) and the tube-loss clock is unmoved ($0.1134$–$0.1146$ vs $0.1134$ soft, the dispersal clock's third booking-robustness confirmation). **The brake-equals-pump crossing does not exist anywhere in $(0, 0.112]$**: the escapement cannot hold the rail because the converged native supply is capped at $\approx 2/3$ of the pump, not because the marginal cell is regulator-obscured. The Section 62 receding-crossing extrapolation and the Section 64 Result 1 controlled-crossing fractions ($2.85 \to 0.47$, a prescribed-hinge quantity) do not transfer to the native release: natively, nothing crosses. The $d_0$-from-survival premise (rail residence by marginal absorption) is CLOSED at the bare-braid level under the declared force law, and the per-click-impulse calibration question DISSOLVES — there is no regulator-independent per-click impulse because there are no clicks; the physical object is the continuous brake rate, and that is converged. (The per-click witness channel confirms the dissolution as designed: the two isolated $t=0$ boundary openings book region-convention-bound impulses, dt-dirty $53$–$87\%$, flagged.)

**Result 3 — the native escapement-under-tilt response is a PUMP; the Section 64 Result 2 damping row is superseded at native grade.** The `--tilt-projection` instrument (the Section 63 prescribed-worldline rate family on the middle layer; impulse-resolved same-source booking only, $a_{\mathrm{frozen}} = 0$, isolating the click channel; both middle sites, cycle-averaged, $\kappa$-scaled torque per unit tilt rate): $P^{\mathrm{click}}_{xx} = +15.9/+15.3/+14.8$ at $\rho_c = 0.022/0.08/0.112$ — **positive (anti-damping) at every stratum**. Witnesses: readout doubling $2.7\%$, quadrature+guard tightening $1.5\%$, baseline transverse null $\sim10^{-7}$; the rate-halving witness reveals the response is NONLINEAR ($\dot\eta = 0.01$ doubles the coefficient): a fixed-magnitude, rate-sign-following torque $\approx +0.3$ ($\kappa$-scaled) along the tilt rate — a dry-friction-type pump whose equivalent linear gain GROWS as amplitude shrinks. Sign robust across all cells and witnesses. The Section 64 Result 2 estimate ($d_{\mathrm{click}} \approx 0.29$–$3.2$ of middle-diagonal DAMPING through the $S<0$ chain at the Rows 7/8 click bands) rested on the artifact click train and the sign model; natively the channel does not damp the middle diagonal — it pumps it. **Joint verdict (the Sections 62/63 standing questions, both answered natively).** (i) No clean marginal brake-equals-pump cell exists — the map is regulator-clean and the crossing is absent because the converged supply caps at $\approx 2/3$ pump; (ii) the bare braid cannot supply its own axis absorber — the click channel's native tilt response is anti-damping, composing with the anti-damping wake block (Section 63) and the pencil saturation rows (Sections 64–65). The bare spindle braid requires the environment on BOTH retention channels, with two converged quantitative targets: the tangential deficit $\ge 1/3$ of the rail pump at the rail, and the axis absorber $d^* \approx 1.02$ isotropic-class (Sections 64–65 refinement: $d_{I,O} \approx 0.5$-class on inner/caps IF a middle absorber exists — natively the middle click channel supplies none, so the sea's target reverts toward the full $d^*$-class and additionally must overcome the fixed-magnitude $\approx 0.3$ click pump on the middle at finite tilt-rate amplitude).

**Durable positives retained at priority level.** The impulse-resolved native booking (regulator-converged absorbed-fraction rows; the `atol`/`rtol` + tangency-guard two-regulator honesty protocol with halving witnesses; adopted per Section 64 route (c) — the native run and any successor row now book on it); the converged bare brake ceiling $\approx 0.67$ pump at the V5 seed (a native constant of the bare channel); the artifact diagnosis with the poised-window ladder-vs-event cross-check as the reusable discriminator; the native fixed-magnitude rate-sign-following click-pump row ($\approx +0.3$, a native nonlinear-torque constant); the dispersal-clock booking-robustness (third confirmation).

**Instrument caveats, declared.** The tangency guard $g = 2\times10^{-3}$ is a regulator (halving witness $3.9\%$; the $g \to 0$ limit is not established); the within-window receiver advance is frozen-per-accepted-step (matches the refined-ladder ODE reference to $\le 0.4\%$; a convention, not a proof); the tilt projection is prescribed-worldline seed grade (frozen geometry, middle layer only — the cap/inner same-source channels are natively closed) and its coefficient is an equivalent linearization at the declared rate step; holds are $0.25$ rotations (the Rows 7/8 convention).

## 67. The Sea-Lane Axis-Absorber Estimate: the Linear Orientational-Dipole Sea Is Band-Structured but STARVES the Inner, Its Block Is Circulatory-Dominated, and Scaling It Up Destabilizes — the Linear-Response Sea Route Closes; the Drift-Orientation Torque Is the Named Surviving Hypothesis (2026-07-10)

Executing the Section 66 next closure goal (operator thread). Owner script extended in place ([spindle-support-ratio-targeted-search.mjs](../../../../scripts/braid-ideal/spindle-support-ratio-targeted-search.mjs), `seaTiltDampingEstimate`; tests 24 passing). Model: six octahedral orientational-dipole members ($p_0$ from the braid dipole) at $R_{\mathrm{sea}}$, relaxing at rate $\gamma$ toward the braid's causally delayed field; the full loop chain per member — layer-$L'$ tilt $\to$ per-braid-site delayed field-direction modulation at the member (moving-source exact causal roots, per-sample phases) $\to$ orientational response $\gamma/(\gamma+i\omega)$ $\to$ per-site delayed reaction torque on layer $L$ (static-endpoint exact delays, receiver-normal factor) — assembled in the frequency domain so every pair carries its own causal phase (the Row 3 discipline; no uniform-loop-delay idealization). Output: the sea's complex tilt chain $C_{LL'}(\omega)$, split as stiffness $\mathrm{Re}\,C$ and velocity block $\mathrm{Im}\,C/\omega$, insertable into the completed pencil.

**Result 1 — the response is band-structured in the whirl frequency, as the causal-delay physics predicts.** At $R=3.4$, $\gamma=1$: strongly anti-damping at low whirl frequency (middle diagonal $+3.4$ at $\omega=0.06$, $+1.3$ at $0.28$), a damping band near $\omega\approx0.46$ (diagonals $-0.014/-0.37/-0.073$ for I/M/O), anti-damping again at $1.16$, weakly damping at $2.41$. The band tunes with spacing ($R=3.25$ damps at $\omega=0.2$–$0.46$; $R=4.25$ at $0.38$–$0.46$) — spacing-selective, in bands, existing only because of causal delay: the same commensurability physics as the radial channel.

**Result 2 — the sea starves the inner's tilt sector everywhere probed.** Across $R\in[2.33,4.25]$, $\gamma\in\{1,2\}$, $\omega\in[0.06,2.41]$, the inner diagonal never exceeds $0.09$ in magnitude and sits below $0.02$ in the damping bands — two orders under the requirement class. The axial-organization theorem extends from the radial sector to the tilt sector: an axially organized environment cannot grip the deep inner layer.

**Result 3 — the block is circulatory-dominated and scaling it up destabilizes.** The best-cell block ($R=3.4$, $\gamma=1$, $\omega=0.46$) carries off-diagonal entries dwarfing its diagonals (O$\leftarrow$M $-0.96$, I$\leftarrow$M $+0.37$ against M$\leftarrow$M $-0.37$). Inserted into the completed pencil with the native click pump on the middle: growth WORSENS at physical scale ($+0.389\to+0.437$) and monotonically at $3\times$, $10\times$, $30\times$ coupling (saturating $\approx+0.84$) — the linear sea's tilt response is not a damper with a magnitude problem; it is a circulatory forcing with a small damping by-product. Caveat rows, declared: single-frequency block insertion (the true response is frequency-dependent; the low-$\omega$ behavior is more anti-damping, so the honest self-consistent verdict is not better); linear small-angle orientational response; slow-limit baseline alignment; estimate grade throughout.

**Verdict — the linear orientational-response sea route closes as the axis absorber (scoped negative, estimate grade).** Composing the day: bare wake anti-damping (Section 63), native escapement a rate-sign-following pump (Section 66), off-diagonal click coupling an order short (Section 65), middle-diagonal damping structurally insufficient at any strength (Section 64), and now the linear responsive sea destabilizing at any coupling. Every measured or estimated axis-absorber channel of the RESTING V5-class braid fails. Remaining routes, named: (i) saturated orientational response (the channel that closed the radial 6% class — with the standing caveat that saturated environments are axially organized); (ii) co-orbital cage dynamics; (iii) bulk-boundary medium; and (iv) — new, captured at hypothesis level — **the drift-orientation torque as the moving family's axis restoring channel**: the measured closure anisotropy of the drifting spindle braid (Sections 28–29) is an orientation torque toward axis-parallel motion that exists ONLY at drift, is absent from every resting-frame pencil built so far, and grows with $u$ toward the preferred basin $u^*\approx0.5$–$0.65$. Axis stability may be a property of the MOVING family — matter never rests relative to its sea — and the resting-frame flutter verdicts would then be statements about a state nature does not occupy.

Fail-closed: `retainedBranchClaim=false`, `scoreMovement=no_score_increase`; estimate-grade instruments on prescribed worldlines; nothing here authorizes a release.

Next closure goal: the drifting-family gyroscopic-circulatory pencil — rebuild $M$, $G$, $\Gamma$, $K$, and the delay-memory block on the screw-drifting family at $u$ in the preferred basin, with the measured orientation-torque anisotropy entering the tilt stiffness, and read whether the drift channel supplies the axis restoring force the resting braid lacks (a yes reframes the release protocol itself: candidates should be released MOVING).

## 68. The Drifting-Family Axis Pencil: the Orientation Torque Is Real and Breaks the Global-Tilt Null, but It Stiffens the GLOBAL Axis Orientation, NOT the Internal Flutter — the Moving Family Is Inertia-Anchored AND Globally Axis-Anchored, Yet Still Flutters; the Axis Sector Is Closed at Seed Grade in BOTH Frames (2026-07-10)

Executing the Section 67 next closure goal (operator thread). Owner script extended in place ([spindle-support-ratio-targeted-search.mjs](../../../../scripts/braid-ideal/spindle-support-ratio-targeted-search.mjs): `driftSupportRatios`, `driftRailPinnedEquilibrium`, `driftFixedPoint`, `driftAxisPencil`, `driftVerdictLadder`; tests 29 passing on this owner suite), consuming the screw-drift evaluator ([spindle-braid-screw-drift-evaluator.mjs](../../../../scripts/braid-ideal/spindle-braid-screw-drift-evaluator.mjs)) read-only. The pencil is rebuilt on the SCREW-DRIFTING family: helical worldlines drifting at $u$ along the lab $z$-axis (the aligned spin axis), pinned cadence $c=\sqrt{1-u^2}=1/\gamma$, frozen $\kappa$; a tilt is applied to the internal circular motion with the drift held along fixed lab $z$, so a global tilt is a misalignment of the spin axis RELATIVE to the drift — exactly the Sections 28–29 orientation-torque coordinate. The Sections 28–29 closure anisotropy therefore enters through the MEASURED $K(u)$, not by hand.

**The moving fixed point $V_5(u)$ (deliverable 1).** `driftFixedPoint` alternates the drift rail-pinned radial Newton (frozen $\kappa$, $\gamma$-scaled rail pin — the speed pin is the size pin at drift too) with tangential angle-descent. At $u=0$ it reproduces $V_5$ to the digit (derived size $R_M^{\mathrm{eq}}=3.494\,\kappa\varepsilon^2/c_f^2$, radial basin, $\tau\approx0$). At $u=0.2$ there is a GENUINE moving fixed point with a rail-pinned radial basin (residual $<10^{-3}$, $\tau_I,\tau_O<0.02$, $R_M^{\mathrm{eq}}\approx3.14$) — the moving braid self-equilibrates radially at drift, first native-adjacent witness. In the closure basin ($u\gtrsim0.5$) the radial Newton runs away from the frozen $V_5$ shape (the drifting geometry's dish/cap angles run strongly, Sections 25–26); the pencil verdict there is read on both the closure-optimal (`pass2Optimize`) geometry and the frozen $V_5$ shape and is geometry-robust (below). Declared gap: a fully re-converged $V_5(u)$ radial fixed point in the deep basin needs the per-$u$ angle re-optimization the ledger objective does not yet drive.

**The drift null structure (stated and validated BEFORE any spectrum claim).** At $u=0$, empty-space isotropy makes both global tilts exact nulls of $K_{\mathrm{eff}}=K-\Gamma$: a double zero root, deflated (the Section 61 discipline; reproduced here at `nullCount=2`, $\kappa_{Gx}=\kappa_{Gy}=0$). At drift, tilting the spin axis away from the drift costs closure in EVERY transverse direction equally (residual axisymmetry about the drift axis), so the global-tilt subspace acquires an ISOTROPIC restoring stiffness $k(u)>0$ — the orientation torque — and the double null BREAKS: `nullCount` drops to $0$, the former zero pair lifts into a global nutation/precession pair. The only exact symmetry left, rigid rotation about the drift axis, acts trivially on the drift-fixed tilt coordinates, so no residual exact tilt null survives; the quotient discipline becomes: count roots at numerical zero and deflate exactly those. Validation rows all pass at drift: $z$-rotation covariance $\sim10^{-15}$, cross-block row sums equal the baseline pump ($<10^{-3}$), Durand–Kerner residual $\sim10^{-16}$, $u=0$ cell reproduces the resting completed pencil (Section 63) to $10^{-6}$ ($+0.199$, whirl $2.41$).

**Result 1 — the orientation torque is real, grows as Sections 28–29 predict, and is near-isotropic.** $k(u)=\kappa_{Gx}$: $0$ / $0.031$ / $0.121$ / $0.325$ / $0.496$ / $0.485$ at $u=0/0.1/0.2/0.35/0.5/0.6$ — quadratic at small $u$ (the perpendicular-loss/parallel-cost coefficient of Section 29), peaking in the closure basin $u^*\approx0.5$ then declining toward the photon limit. Near-isotropic ($\kappa_{Gy}\approx\kappa_{Gx}$, residual $\lesssim0.05$, the axisymmetry). Fore-aft/helicity asymmetry booked (odd in $u$): in the preferred electrino-leading direction the stiffness is LARGER ($\kappa_{Gx}=0.71$ at $u=-0.5$ vs $0.50$ at $+0.5$). **The moving family is globally axis-anchored: the drift pins the braid's spin-axis ORIENTATION to its line of motion (the rifle-bullet effect, now a measured restoring stiffness), from the same closure landscape that gives it a finite optimal speed (Section 26). Inertia-anchoring and global axis-anchoring are one mechanism.**

**Result 2 — but the orientation torque does NOT reach the flutter; the axis sector still grows at every $u$.** The gyroscopic-circulatory flutter is a fast internal whirl ($|\lambda|\approx0.2+2.4i$, the middle-dominated/inner-participating RELATIVE-tilt mode of Section 61), which lives in the internal tilt sector orthogonal to the global mode the orientation torque stiffens. Max growth rate across the grid: $+0.199$ / $+0.203$ / $+0.217$ / $+0.237$ / $+0.222$ / $+0.177$ at $u=0/0.1/0.2/0.35/0.5/0.6$ — it rises to $u\approx0.35$ then declines but NEVER crosses zero in the basin. The decline past $u\approx0.6$ tracks $k(u)$ AND the gyroscopic angular momentum both vanishing (whirl $\to0.03$ by $u=0.8$) as the family dissolves toward the photon limit where closure itself climbs (Section 26) — not a restoring mechanism. Geometry-robust: on the $u=0.2$ drift fixed-point geometry $+0.202$; on the closure-optimal `pass2` geometry the flutter is WORSE ($+0.47$ at $u=0.5$); in the preferred drift direction WORSE ($+0.307$ at $u=-0.5$). Pump-robust: the native click pump ($+0.3$ middle diagonal) worsens it ($+0.40$ class); the pump-absorbed counterfactual barely moves it ($+0.21$–$0.26$) — pump-independent, exactly as the resting Section 61. Witnesses: $N_t=8/16$ within $7\times10^{-3}$; rate-step halved within $10^{-5}$.

**Verdict — drift does NOT stabilize the axis sector (scoped negative, seed grade).** The Section 67 surviving hypothesis is answered: the drift-orientation torque exists, is restoring, breaks the global-tilt null, and anchors the moving family's global axis orientation — but it stiffens the WRONG mode. The internal gyroscopic-circulatory flutter that kills the resting braid (Sections 61–66) is untouched and persists at $+0.14$–$0.24$ across the entire physical drift basin, in both drift signs, on both frozen and re-optimized geometry, with and without the pump. The resting-frame flutter verdict CARRIES to the moving family. Composing the arc: the axis sector is now closed at seed grade in BOTH frames — resting (Sections 61–66: every bare channel a pump, the linear sea circulatory-dominated) AND moving (this section: the orientation torque reaches only the global mode). **The rigid uniaxial spindle family cannot self-stabilize its axis in any state of uniform motion; axis confinement requires either non-rigid axis dynamics (internal deformation modes coupling to the flutter, outside every rigid-layer pencil built so far) or the remaining nonlinear/structured sea routes (saturated orientational response, co-orbital cage, bulk-boundary medium).** The reframed-release hope (candidates released MOVING) is retired: a moving release faces the same flutter.

**Durable positives retained at priority level.** The drift pencil instrument (reduces to the resting completed pencil at $u=0$ to $10^{-6}$; the adaptive null-count deflation that measures the broken-null transition); the measured orientation-torque stiffness $k(u)$ as a restoring GLOBAL axis-orientation block (the rifle-bullet effect quantified, near-isotropic, helicity-odd — a corpus-grade statement that the moving family is inertia- and globally-axis-anchored by one closure mechanism); the moving rail-pinned radial fixed point at $u=0.2$ (the speed pin is the size pin at drift); the global-vs-internal mode separation (why an orientation torque that grows to the $0.5$-class leaves a $0.2$-class flutter untouched); and the two-frame axis-sector closure composed.

Fail-closed: `retainedBranchClaim=false`, `scoreMovement=no_score_increase`; prescribed-worldline seed grade (cycle-averaged rigid-layer reduction; frozen-per-$u$ geometry in the deep basin; kinematic + delay-memory blocks only); nothing here authorizes a release or a moving release. Corpus destinations updated: the spindle scene motion/confinement section ([spindle-braid.md](../../../../content/markdown/aaa/noether-braid/spindle-braid.md)) and the bridge's gyroscopic subsection ([angular-momentum-and-spin.md](../../../../content/markdown/aaa/philosophy-history/theory-bridges/angular-momentum-and-spin.md#spindle-braid-spin-scaffold)).

Next closure goal: operator decision — (a) the non-rigid axis-dynamics route: admit an internal deformation coordinate (the shape/breathing mode that the radial basin already carries) and measure its coupling to the flutter whirl — the one channel no rigid-layer pencil can see; or (b) the saturated-orientational-response sea absorber at the confining band (the Section 67 route (i), the channel that closed the radial 6% class), now the leading environment candidate with the two-frame bare closure behind it; or (c) the co-orbital cage dynamics (Section 55 reciprocity), the remaining structured-environment route.

## 69. The Super-Field Inner-Binary Feasibility Resolution: the Nested-Shell Hinge Route Cannot Supply the Middle Rail-Pump Deficit — Cross-Hit Relay Closed for the Neutral Braid, the Super-Field Inner Injects a New Pump, and the Native Seed Cannot Represent It; S1/S2 Reroutes to the Section 68 Internal-Deformation Channel (2026-07-10)

Resolving the Section 10 crux directly against the S1/S2 target (the shape-attractor / tangential rail-pump absorber; the Row 7 blocker `rail_pump_unabsorbed_coherent_expansion`, Section 60). This section books **no new instrument**: it composes the already-converged Sections 8/10/11/12/14 nested-shell results and the Section 66 native brake ceiling against the middle rail-pump deficit, and adds one native-seed representability check. Central solver untouched; no solver or runner writes; the 29-test native-run suite and the drift-envelope suites stay green.

**The target.** Middle rail pump $+0.2274$ (Section 60, `DECLARED.row8.pumpDeclared`). The native impulse-resolved self-hit brake caps at $\approx2/3$ of the pump at every stratum ($0.667\to0.494$, no marginal cell in $(0,0.112]$, dt-clean; Section 66 Result 2). The residual **tangential deficit is $\approx0.076$ ($\approx1/3$ pump)** and requires an environment (Section 66 joint verdict).

**Result 1 — the cross-hit relay inner$\to$middle is closed for the neutral braid (measurement, converged).** The only channel by which a nested inner binary could deposit its absorption onto the middle receiver is the cross-hit hinge. On the causal root-sum (Section 14, converged $0.214\to0.220\to0.224$ under $N_T=2000\to8000$) a neutral inner binary cancels $\approx97\%$: net $\approx+0.035$, about $1\%$ of the pump and **ejective** (wrong sign). A fully polarity-imbalanced (non-neutral, inadmissible) inner shell reaches only $\sim20\%$ of the pump (Section 14 Result 3) — below the $33\%$ deficit and outside neutrality.

**Result 2 — a super-field inner layer injects a new, stronger un-absorbed pump (derivation + measurement).** Driving the inner layer super-field ($\beta_{\mathrm{in}}>1$, the Section 8/9 straddling gate) turns on the partner pump on that layer, which *grows* above field speed ($\Phi_{\mathrm{tan}}=2.9\to4.3$ across $\beta=1\to1.4$; Section 11 Result 1). Its own terminal absorber is the self-hit, which natively caps at $2/3$ (Section 66) — so the inner layer coherently-expands like the middle. The field-speed pin (Sections 11/12) closes each shell's *own* budget; it is not an outward relay. Adding the shell therefore *worsens* the radial ledger rather than closing the middle deficit.

**Result 3 — the native V5 seed cannot represent the configuration.** `SELF_EQUILIBRATED_V5` is iso-frequency: all three layers (I/M/O) share one $\omega$, differing only in radius and tilt ([spindle-support-ratio-targeted-search.mjs](../../../../scripts/braid-ideal/spindle-support-ratio-targeted-search.mjs) `SELF_EQUILIBRATED_V5`). By Section 8 Result 1 an iso-frequency braid produces zero cross-hit clicks natively; the middle's only same-source channel is its marginal self-hit at $\beta_M=1$. A distinct super-field inner frequency with sustained coplanar hinge alignment is a new two-frequency native construction — the Sections 8–14 nested studies all ran on separate prescribed-worldline evaluators, never on the central solver — so a native release of this configuration is not a Row 7/8 reuse and, by Results 1–2, would spend a candidate row against a route three converged instruments predict closed.

**Verdict:** `nested_shell_super_field_inner_binary_cannot_supply_middle_rail_pump_deficit_route_closed_for_S1S2`. The super-field inner binary is self-consistent only as a field-speed-pinned terminal shell (Sections 11/12, reduced grade); it does **not** supply the $\approx0.076$ middle tangential deficit, and it does not spoil the bare radial ledger only in the trivial sense that it creates a new un-absorbed pump instead of fixing the old one. The residual against the deficit is $\approx-0.05$ (neutral relay $+1\%$ ejective vs. $+33\%$ needed); the geometry knobs that move it (inner polarity imbalance $\to\sim20\%$; radius ratio $q$; $\beta_{\mathrm{in}}$) are all measured insufficient or inadmissible (Section 9 fact 3, Section 14 Result 3). The nested-shell hinge route is closed for S1/S2. Fail-closed: `retainedBranchClaim=false`, `scoreMovement=no_score_increase`, no accepted seed-path certificate, no new validator or schema.

**Reroute (decision-tree branch 3) and shared-channel synthesis (hypothesis).** S1/S2 moves onto the Section 68 non-rigid axis / internal-deformation channel. The rail pump *already* excites exactly such a coordinate: the Row 7 coherent expansion ($R_\perp:0.96\to2.37$, Section 60) is the shape/breathing mode running away because the pump feeds it. Section 68 routes the axis flutter to the same route (a) fork. So the internal-deformation coordinate the radial basin already carries is the single **shared** surviving channel for both S1/S2 (rail-pump absorption) and the axis-anchor coherence — the two "separate environment channels" of Section 66 plausibly close together once that mode is un-frozen. Claim level: Results 1–3 are derivation + measurement (converged instruments); the shared-channel synthesis is hypothesis grade.

Next closure goal: scope the Section 68 route (a) internal-deformation instrument — admit the breathing/shape coordinate the radial basin carries (not a rigid-layer pencil) and measure its coupling to (i) the $+0.2274$ rail pump (does it absorb the $\approx0.076$ deficit into a bounded internal oscillation instead of coherent expansion) and (ii) the gyroscopic-circulatory flutter whirl ($|\lambda|\approx0.2+2.4i$, Section 61) — as the shared closure for S1/S2 and the axis-anchor coherence. Build spec written: [nonrigid-axis-internal-deformation-instrument-spec.md](nonrigid-axis-internal-deformation-instrument-spec.md) — the coupled 18-degree breathing–flutter pencil ($K_{\rm rad}$/$K_{\rm tilt}$ reused; new cross-blocks $C_{rt}$, $C_{tr}$, and the exact spin-transport $\dot J$ rate block $G_{rt}$ that a frozen-radial pencil zeroes), extending `spindle-support-ratio-targeted-search.mjs`, central solver untouched.

## 70. The Tangential-Sea No-Go and the Braid+Sea Complex Reframe: the Structured Sea Is Barred in Principle From the Equatorial Rail-Pump Job, the Iso-Frequency Drop Does Not Self-Absorb, and S1/S2 + the Axis Anchor + $A_0$ Reframe as One Self-Consistent Complex (2026-07-11)

Resolving the fork the route-enumeration cornered: §§61–66 (bare resting), §68 (bare drift), §69 (nested hinge), and §68(a) ([nonrigid-axis-internal-deformation-instrument-spec.md](nonrigid-axis-internal-deformation-instrument-spec.md), Deliverable 2 — the internal-deformation route closed at linear **and** parametric grade) close every bare/internal channel for the S1/S2 rail-pump absorber and the axis anchor, handing both back to a sea already known to be axis-only (§50). This section decides whether the structured sea is structurally barred from the S1/S2 (tangential rail-pump) job before any further structured-sea sweep. It books **no new instrument**: it composes the converged §8/§9/§11/§13/§14 nested results and the Corollary S static-sea certificate against the deficit, and states the reframe. Central solver untouched; no runner/fixture writes; suites unaffected. Operator-reviewed and **accepted** 2026-07-11.

**Result 1 — the Tangential-Sea No-Go (named; exact core + certified + named-bounded).** No sea organized around the braid's polarity/axis (cap) structure supplies the middle **equatorial** rail's tangential deficit ($\gtrsim0.076$, i.e. $\ge1/3$ of the $+0.2274$ pump). The three exhaustive sea classes each close decisively:

1. **Axisymmetric ($m=0$) limit — EXACT bar.** An exactly axisymmetric-about-$\hat n$ source exerts identically zero azimuthal (tangential) force on any co-axial site — rotational symmetry leaves no preferred $\hat\varphi$ direction — instantaneously, a fortiori cycle-averaged. This is the tangential analog of the §50 radial law ("an axially-organized environment cannot push on an equatorial ring") and the §67 tilt-sector law, and it is *exact*, not merely averaged. The cap-organized sea the braid selects is axisymmetric to leading (dipole) order, so this is the physically realized class.
2. **Static azimuthal structure — CERTIFIED non-absorber.** Any static sea (any azimuthal order, any orientation order) has $\Pi_{\rm tan}^{\rm sea}(\beta,\varphi)=c_0(\varphi)-\beta\,Q(\varphi)$ exactly (sub-field branch weight is linear in $\beta$; Corollary S, [delayed-escape-certificate-lemma-proof-packet.md](delayed-escape-certificate-lemma-proof-packet.md)): the geometric torque $c_0$ has **exactly zero cycle-average** (odd about the $C_3$ phases; cyclic mean $\le4\times10^{-17}$) — it re-phases within a cycle and does zero net cyclic work, never a brake — and the velocity-linear $Q$ (the only along-velocity channel) is interval-certified $\sup|Q|\le0.275\ll c_1=2.881$, i.e. removes $<10\%$ of the pump (cyclic mean $\approx2.4\%$), **order-robust** across aligned / conjugate / paired-antiphase ($Q\equiv0$) / isotropic-disorder.
3. **Dynamical azimuthal response — the ONE unbarred channel, named and independently bounded closed.** The only net-braking channel is a sea responding *dissipatively* (in quadrature) at the braid's drive harmonic (the $m=3$ threefold) — its orientational AC susceptibility $\chi''(3\omega)$ at the rotation frequency. Bounded shut by two independent instruments plus a no-decoupling lemma: induced polarization needs $\chi''\gtrsim8$–$19$, a factor $5$–$15$ above the cluster orientational stiffness the sea supplies (Group-A item 1 / [sh-0-sea-diagnostic-candidate-model.md](sh-0-sea-diagnostic-candidate-model.md)); the §67 linear responsive sea is circulatory-dominated, starves the inner, and destabilizes at any coupling ($+0.389\to+0.437$ at physical scale, saturating $\approx+0.84$ at $30\times$); and the cage-symmetry scan proves the azimuthal multipole that carries any tangential grip is the **same multipole** that injects the transverse shear spoiling $\sigma$, cap-latitude-organized — grip and shear cannot be separated, and neither reaches the equatorial rail with a brake's sign+phase.

Verdict `structured_sea_barred_in_principle_from_equatorial_rail_pump_S1S2`: the exact $m=0$ bar covers the realized class; the static and dynamical extensions each close order-robustly; the one unbarred channel ($\chi''(3\omega)$ at $m=3$) is the *already-closed* induced-polarization channel, so **no new sea sweep is warranted**. This does not contradict the sea's Row-4 tangential feed ($+0.117$ native): that authority is on the **tilted, off-equatorial** inner and is directed at angular momentum / orientation, not the equatorial rail. The sea remains the angular-momentum bath, the cap support, and the off-equatorial / global axis-orientation anchor — **not** the equatorial tangential rail-pump scaffold. Claim level: (1) exact; (2) interval-certified (declared rounding model) + exact $c_0$ cancellation; (3) measurement (two converged instruments) + the exact cage-scan lemma.

**Result 2 — the iso-frequency drop does not self-absorb (refuted; no native row warranted).** Testing whether a non-iso-frequency (distinct layer $\omega$, rigid shared-axis) braid reopens a nonvanishing inner$\to$middle cross-hit relay supplying $\approx0.076$ internally, the premise "the relay vanishes ONLY because V5 is iso-frequency" is **false**. The distinct-frequency family bifurcates, both branches already quantified on converged prescribed-worldline evaluators: (a) a **sub-field** distinct-$\omega$ inner gives **exactly zero** cross-hit clicks (§8/§9 straddle gate — a sub-field inner never projects its velocity onto a sightline at $c_f$), so distinct frequency alone reopens nothing; (b) a **super-field** inner reopens clicks but **neutrality forces $\approx97\%$ cancellation** to a converged net $\approx+0.035$ ($\sim1\%$ of the pump, *ejective*; §14 causal root-sum), a charged/imbalanced inner reaches only $\sim20\%$ (below the $33\%$ deficit **and** inadmissible), and the super-field inner **injects its own strengthening partner pump** ($\Phi_{\rm tan}\,2.92\to4.28$, §11; §69), worsening the ledger — with frequency-ratio saturation removing any gain from raw $\omega_{\rm in}$ (§9). The binding barriers are **neutrality** and the **super-field straddle**, not iso-frequency. Verdict `iso_frequency_drop_does_not_self_absorb_binding_barriers_are_neutrality_and_super_field_straddle`. No new two-frequency native row is warranted (§69 already: "a candidate row against a route three converged instruments predict closed").

**Result 3 — the reframe (the selected branch): S1/S2 + the axis anchor + $A_0$ are one self-consistent braid+sea complex.** With every local channel for the $+0.076$ equatorial deficit closed (bare self-hit caps $2/3$ even super-field §66; cross-hit ejective §14; §68(a) breathing closed; sea barred, Result 1), the bare-release premise is the artifact. The "$+0.076$ tangential deficit" is a **local equatorial-rail** quantity computed with three coordinates frozen — $\beta_M$ pinned exactly at $c_f$, the internal-deformation (breathing/shape) coordinate frozen, and the sea passive. Result 1 forbids a **local equatorial** sea brake but **not** a **global angular-momentum drain** through the off-equatorial / axis channels the sea provably owns (Row-4 $+0.117$ inner feed; the §68 orientation torque). The right object is the self-consistent fixed point over $\{$layer radii + size, layer cadences **including the middle rail speed** $\beta_M$, axis orientation $\hat n$, the un-frozen §69 internal-deformation coordinate, the dynamical sea configuration$\}$, solved simultaneously with all sea members' reciprocity ledgers (§55) closing — §54's braid-plus-cage "molecule in a solvent." The open physical question no bare instrument can pose: does the middle's rail-pump angular momentum **transport off the equator** (via the now-dynamical §69 deformation coordinate) into the inner/axis sector where the sea **can** drain it, so the *total* angular-momentum budget closes with no *local* equatorial brake? This is the same object $A_0$ (the mass map) needs — the boosted-delay grand convergence ([../../master-equation-closure/boosted-delay-attractor-theorem-target.md](../../master-equation-closure/boosted-delay-attractor-theorem-target.md)) funnels the large-drift ruler, settled $\sigma=0$, and $A_0$ through one structured-sea axis anchor, and §69's shared-channel synthesis makes the un-frozen deformation coordinate the shared gate for S1/S2 and the axis-anchor coherence. The load-bearing new distinction: a **local equatorial rail brake** (barred, Result 1) versus a **global angular-momentum drain through off-equatorial channels** (open). Claim level: hypothesis (the global-drain resolution) + scoping.

The only nonzero deformation coupling is the **parametric** channel ($d\!\text{flutter}/ds = -0.477$, the spin-transport $dJ_L/ds_L = [1.81, 1.92, 0.58]$), which is velocity-class (acts on the breathing *rate*, oscillatory) and was already closed at §68(a) Deliverable 2.

**The reduced complex escapement (Deliverable 2) confirms the runaway.** Wired with the measured transport ($0$ DC) and drain ($0.0136$), the size mode is a **runaway** (disperses at $t \approx 7.1$ — the Row-7 coherent expansion reproduced); the off-equatorial reservoir overflows ($J_{\rm off}$ climbs to $39.7$ as the parametric channel dumps into it faster than the starved drain removes it, $0.0136$ against the $0.0757$ needed — a $5.5\times$ shortfall). A **positive control** ($C_{rt}$-fraction $\ge 1$ with drain $0.5$) *does* bound the size mode, so the integrator is not rigged to run away — it runs away only at the measured authorities. The block-diagonal control (`coupling = none`) reproduces the same escape residual, so the composition is transparent.

S1/S2 does not close as a coupled complex at this grade; the §69 shared-channel synthesis is falsified at the linear/complex level.

**The load-bearing caveat (what keeps the reframe alive, and the named next probe).** The DC transport gate is exactly zero **at the axisymmetric rest fixed point** (V5, $u=0$ cross-blocks): axisymmetry is *precisely* what forbids the radial$\leftrightarrow$tilt stiffness coupling. A **symmetry-broken** configuration need not: (i) under drift the spin axis anchors off the geometric axis (§68), (ii) the dynamical co-drift cage imprints a discrete azimuthal structure on the braid, and (iii) an anchored-oblique complex breaks the $z\to-z$ and axisymmetry that null $C_{rt}$/$C_{tr}$. The instrument measured the cross-blocks on the **rest** `internalDeformationPencil` (the built one is $u=0$); a **drift / broken-symmetry cross-block build** is the surviving question — whether breaking axisymmetry opens a nonzero DC transport-off-equator gate before the reframe is fully closed. | config | crtNorm | ctrNorm | crtRel | ctrRel | transport frac | reaches $1/3$? |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| rest ($u=0$) | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | no (the §71 null) |
| oblique drift only ($u{=}0.2,\theta{=}90°$) | 0.172 | 0.128 | 0.077 | 0.057 | 0.077 | no |
| anchored off-axis only ($\text{baseTilt}{=}20°$) | 0.059 | **1.185** | 0.026 | **0.528** | 0.528 | **yes** |

| ($u{=}0.5,\theta{=}90°,30°$) | 0.406 | 0.854 | 0.181 | 0.381 | 0.381 | yes |

The transport-off-equator channel is $C_{tr}=\partial T/\partial s$ (a breath of a layer generates a tilt torque — the deformation moving angular momentum from the breathing/rail sector into the tilt/axis sector), opened chiefly by the **anchored-axis base-tilt** (ctrRel $0.53$–$0.59$, *past* the $0.333$ deficit fraction); the oblique drift opens the reciprocal $C_{rt}$ (crtRel $0.08$–$0.18$). Both are exactly zero at the axisymmetric rest fixed point because axisymmetry/reflection forbids them — and breaking that symmetry, exactly as the physical anchored-oblique complex does, opens them.

**The four-gate ledger at the anchored-oblique config ($u{=}0.35,\theta{=}90°,\text{baseTilt}{=}30°$).** Pump $+0.2274$; local self-hit brake $-0.1517$ ($2/3$); **transport-off-equator $= 0.587$** (open, past the deficit); off-equatorial sea drain inner $= 0.0136$ ($\le0.02$, §67); escape residual $\mathbf{+0.0621}$ (escape fraction $0.333\to\mathbf{0.273}$). **The bottleneck MOVES from `transport_off_equator_axisymmetry_forbidden` to `sea_drain_inner_starved`.** Transport now delivers $\approx0.587\times0.0757\approx0.044$ of angular momentum to the off-equatorial sector, but the sea's *dissipative* off-equatorial authority drains only $\approx0.014$, so $\approx0.062$ still escapes — capped now by the drain, not the transport.

**Verdict — the §71 "transport fails" branch is REFUTED; the §70 reframe is RE-OPENED (`broken_symmetry_opens_transport_gate_past_deficit_bottleneck_moves_to_sea_drain`).** The transport-off-equator gate is null *only* at the axisymmetric rest fixed point; at the physically-relevant anchored-oblique complex it opens well past the $1/3$-pump deficit. S1/S2 is therefore not closed by a barred local brake (§70) nor by a forbidden transport gate (§71) — it lands on the **"transport closes but the drain cannot keep up"** branch: the single remaining blocker is the sea's off-equatorial **dissipative** drain authority (§67: inner-starved $\le0.02$, circulatory-dominated), a sharply and correctly posed *global* drain-shortfall target rather than the barred *local* brake. The conservative §68 orientation-torque anchoring ($k(u)$ up to $\approx0.5$) is what *supplies* the base-tilt that opens the gate, but it does zero net cyclic work and is not itself the drain.

**Caveats (declared, seed grade).** (i) The cross-blocks are measured self-consistently at the broken-symmetry geometry, but the diagonal pencil blocks are not re-measured there, so only the cross-block **gate** is claimed — not the full coupled spectrum at the anchored-oblique point. (ii) The base-tilt (anchored angle) is imposed ($20$–$30°$), physically justified by the §68 torque $\hat n\to\hat d$ but not yet solved self-consistently with the opened transport. (iii) The new bottleneck is the sea's **dissipative** drain; whether the §67 inner-starvation is intrinsic or liftable at the anchored-oblique config (where the sea sees a tilted braid) is the open question.

## 73. The Global Drain-Shortfall Probe (the Last Gate): the Transport Opens but the Off-Equatorial DISSIPATIVE Sea Drain Stays Inner-Starved at the Tilted Braid — Angle-Robustly ~10x Short; the Coupled Complex Does NOT Close at Seed Grade, and the Blocker Is Now Fully Localized to the Sea's Weak Dissipative Authority (the Same Root as the §70 No-Go) (2026-07-11)

Executing the Section 72 next closure goal (operator-directed). `seaTiltDampingEstimate` gained a global `baseTilt` (the whole braid anchored off the geometric $z$-axis, so the fixed bulk sea sees a **tilted** braid — the anchored-oblique complex, unlike §67's axisymmetric measurement; default $0$ reproduces §67 exactly, test-pinned). New composing probe `globalDrainShortfall` reads, per anchored config, the opened transport ($C_{tr}$, §72) against the sea's off-equatorial **dissipative** drain (the best damping-band inner diagonal at the tilted braid) plus the §68 anchoring stiffness. **Result 1 — the inner-starvation does NOT lift when the sea sees a tilted braid; it persists and worsens.** Best damping-band inner diagonal vs `baseTilt`: $-0.0136$ ($0°$, the §67 value) $\to -0.0036/-0.0039/-0.0041/-0.0043$ at $15°/20°/30°/45°$ — the off-equatorial dissipative drain stays $\le0.02$ (worsens), and the equatorial/middle drain collapses ($-0.366\to-0.001$) as the braid tilts out of the sea's grip. The §67 inner-starvation is intrinsic, not an artifact of the axisymmetric measurement.

**Result 2 — the drain-shortfall is angle-robust: transport opens, drain stays flat, ~94% of the deficit escapes.** The composed book ($\text{railResidual}=0.0757$):

| $u$ | $\theta$ | baseTilt | transport frac | transported | drain | escape | escape / pump | $k_{\rm anchor}$ (§68) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.2 | 90° | 15° | 0.347 | 0.026 | 0.0036 | 0.0721 | 0.317 | 0.121 |
| 0.2 | 90° | 20° | 0.477 | 0.036 | 0.0039 | 0.0718 | 0.316 | 0.121 |
| 0.35 | 90° | 30° | 0.587 | 0.044 | 0.0041 | 0.0716 | 0.315 | 0.325 |
| 0.5 | 90° | 45° | 0.716 | 0.054 | 0.0043 | 0.0714 | 0.314 | 0.496 |

As the anchoring grows (transport fraction $0.35\to0.72$, $k_{\rm anchor}\,0.12\to0.50$ restoring), the dissipative drain stays flat ($\approx0.004$, an order of magnitude below the transported $0.026$–$0.054$), so the escape residual barely moves ($0.072\to0.071$). The §68 anchoring is restoring on every config (an anchored angle **exists**, Piece 2), but because the drain is starved across the whole anchored-angle range, the exact self-consistent angle does not rescue it — the shortfall is **angle-robust**.

**Verdict — the coupled complex does NOT close at seed grade (`drain_starved_at_anchored_complex_transport_opens_but_dissipative_drain_short_reframe_blocked_on_the_drain`).** The full arc: the sea is barred from a **local** equatorial brake (§70); the transport-off-equator gate is **closed** at the axisymmetric rest fixed point (§71) but **opens past the deficit** at the anchored-oblique complex (§72, re-opening the reframe); yet the sea's off-equatorial **dissipative** drain the No-Go leaves open is **inner-starved** ($\approx0.004$, ~10x short of the $\approx0.044$ transported), angle-robustly (§73). The transported angular momentum reaches the off-equatorial sector but has nowhere dissipative to go, so the complex does not reach steady state — the Row-7 coherent expansion survives. The blocker is now **fully localized and quantified**: a factor-$\approx10$ shortfall in the sea's off-equatorial dissipative (orientational-damping) authority.

**Consilience (hypothesis grade) — the same root cause as the §70 No-Go.** The §70 Tangential-Sea No-Go bounded the sea's *dissipative* authority on the equatorial channel closed (the $\chi''(3\omega)$ orientational susceptibility is $5$–$15\times$ too weak because the cluster orientation stiffness is too high). §73 finds the *off-equatorial* dissipative drain is $\approx10\times$ too weak. Both are the sea's dissipative orientational response, so plausibly one root cause: **the sea's dissipative (χ″) authority is intrinsically ~an order of magnitude too weak, whether asked to brake the equatorial rail (barred) or drain the off-equatorial sector (starved).** The §72 transport reframe genuinely opened a new channel, but it delivers angular momentum to a drain limited by the same weak χ″. What the transport reframe changes is *where* the blocker sits (a quantified off-equatorial drain shortfall, not a barred local brake) — a correctly-posed, sharp residual — but it does not close the complex at seed grade.

**Surviving routes beyond seed grade (named, not opened).** The measured drain is the §67 **linear** responsive sea (relaxing dipoles). Not measured here: (i) a **saturated / nonlinear** orientational sea response (§67 route (i)); (ii) a genuinely **co-orbital cage** (§55) with its own angular-momentum sink (trading the drained angular momentum against orbital motion rather than dissipating it orientationally) — which could be a *conservative* transfer to the cage's orbit, sidestepping the weak χ″; (iii) a **native retained-history** sea back-reaction differing from the seed-grade linear estimate. Route (ii) is the most distinct from the bounded χ″ channel and is the natural next probe.

## 74. The Co-Orbital-Cage Angular-Momentum-Sink Probe (the Last Surviving Route, Closed): a Conservative Co-Orbiting Cage Transfers ZERO Secular Tangential Angular Momentum — Secular Shedding Requires Dissipation, Which Is the Same χ″ the No-Go Bounds; the Braid+Sea Complex Has No Closed Route for S1/S2 at Seed Grade (2026-07-11)

Executing the Section 73 next closure goal (operator-directed). New probe `coOrbitalCageSink`: the §55 octahedral cage is made to **co-orbit** about the braid axis at rate $\Omega_{\rm orbit}$ (members MOVING, carried on the delayed force law with the moving-source branch weight $D_s=c_f-\mathbf v_{\rm orbit}\cdot\hat{\mathbf r}$), and the cycle-averaged (secular) tangential force on the middle equatorial rail is measured — the angular-momentum-transfer channel by which the cage's orbit could absorb the $\approx0.044$ residual conservatively. CLI `--co-orbital-sink`; owner suite $44/44$. Central solver untouched; seed grade; fail-closed.

**Result — co-orbital motion opens NO secular tangential sink.** Secular tangential force on the rail (as a fraction of the pump) vs orbit rate $\Omega_{\rm orbit}/\omega \in \{0,0.25,0.5,1.0,1.5\}$: $\{0,\,0.0009,\,0,\,0,\,0\}$ — a maximum of **0.09% of the pump**, *below* even the static Corollary-S bound ($\le10\%$), and far below the $0.58$ of the residual a sink would need. The static anchor ($\Omega_{\rm orbit}=0$) gives **exactly zero** secular tangential (the Corollary-S $c_0$ cyclic-cancellation reproduced), while the secular **radial** pull is nonzero and grows with orbit rate ($-0.028\to-0.101$) — the cage couples radially but not tangentially. Verdict `co_orbital_cage_secular_tangential_stays_within_static_corollary_s_bound_no_conservative_sink`.

**Why (the decisive structural reason).** Secular (steady, cycle-averaged) angular-momentum transfer between the rotating braid and an external object requires a **dissipative** (phase-lagged) coupling; a **conservative** coupling gives a reactive torque that oscillates with zero cycle average (the $c_0$ result, Corollary S). A co-orbiting **neutral** cage adds orbital velocity to the branch weight but its antipodal $\pm$ endpoints cancel the tangential contribution to $\approx0.09\%$ (the same neutrality cancellation as §14's $97\%$ and Corollary S's $c_0$). So the cage's orbital motion does **not** create a new conservative secular channel — it provides a reservoir that only a dissipative torque could fill, and that dissipative torque is the same χ″-class coupling §70/§73 bound $\approx10\times$ too weak. A "conservative co-orbital sink" for a *continuous* pump is structurally empty: the transfer that would fill it is dissipative.

**Synthesis — the full arc, and the complete seed-grade verdict on the §70 reframe.** Every named route for S1/S2 (the middle rail-pump absorber) is now measured or bounded closed:

| route | disposition | section |
|---|---|---|
| bare self-hit brake | caps at $2/3$ (even super-field) | §66 |
| nested super-field inner (cross-hit) | neutral relay ejective / super-field injects pump | §69 |
| internal deformation (breathing) | linear axisymmetry-forbidden, parametric no bounded cycle | §68(a) |
| **local** equatorial sea brake | barred in principle (exact $m{=}0$ + static $Q<10\%$ + dynamical χ″ $5$–$15\times$ short) | §70 |
| transport off-equator (rest) | axisymmetry-forbidden ($C_{rt}=C_{tr}=0$) | §71 |
| transport off-equator (anchored-oblique) | **OPENS** past the deficit | §72 |
| off-equatorial dissipative drain | inner-starved $\approx10\times$ short, angle-robust | §73 |
| conservative co-orbital sink | zero secular transfer | §74 |

The coupled braid+sea complex does **not** close S1/S2 at seed grade. The §72 transport reframe was real and important — it moved the blocker from a barred local brake to a sharply quantified off-equatorial sink shortfall — but both a dissipative drain (§73) and a conservative sink (§74) for that transported angular momentum are closed, and by the same root: **secular angular-momentum shedding from the braid to any braid-organized, neutral, orientational environment is χ″-limited, and the sea's χ″ is intrinsically $\approx10\times$ too weak** (the cluster orientation stiffness is too high, §70 Result 1). This is the unifying finding of the §70–§74 arc.

**Claim level and the honest conclusion.** All of §70–§74 are seed-grade / prescribed-worldline / linear / neutral-environment estimates (converged instruments, exact where marked). Two doors remain, both outside this grade: (i) a **native retained-history** braid+sea back-reaction could differ from the linear estimates (nonlinear/saturated channels the seed grade cannot see); (ii) a genuinely **new ontological ingredient** — a formation-history or reaction channel, a non-neutral environment component, or an environment response not governed by the cluster-stiffness-limited orientational χ″. Absent one of these, S1/S2 has no closed route under the rigid+deformation+dynamical-neutral-sea ontology, which is itself a sharp, falsifiable statement about what the theory's next ingredient must supply. Fail-closed: `retainedBranchClaim=false`, `scoreMovement=no_score_increase`; no release, no new schema.

Run records: `.tmp/braid-ideal/coupled-complex/co-orbital-sink.json`. Reproduce: `node scripts/braid-ideal/spindle-support-ratio-targeted-search.mjs --co-orbital-sink --pretty`.

Next closure goal: lift the seed-grade ceiling on the localized blocker — either (a) a **native retained-history** co-drifting-cage back-reaction run (does a nonlinear/saturated sea response exceed the linear χ″ estimate the whole §70–§74 arc rests on), or (b) scope the **new-ingredient** fork the arc has cornered (formation-history / reaction channel / non-neutral environment) — the theory-level decision now that the rigid+deformation+dynamical-neutral-sea ontology is measured, at seed grade, to lack a sink for the middle rail pump.

## 75. Door (a) — the Native Fail-Closed Gate: a Nonlinear/Saturable Cage χ″ Does NOT Beat the Linear Estimate (Multiple $\approx1.06$, Not the $\approx10\times$ Needed) — the Local-Sink No-Go Is Sealed Above Seed Grade, and (b) the Radiation-Reaction Reframe Proceeds With an Airtight Predecessor (2026-07-11)

Prepending the operator-directed fail-closed gate before finalizing the (b) radiation-reaction reframe: the one measurement that decides whether the §70–§74 local-sink no-go is airtight or whether a nonlinear/saturated *native* sea response beats the linear χ″ estimate the entire arc rests on. New probe `nativeSaturatedCageDrain` (CLI `--native-drain-gate`) lifts the §73 drain off its linear, prescribed-worldline χ″ basis: the cage member's orientational response is integrated as a **saturable nonlinear unit-dipole rotator** ($|\hat p|=1$ *is* the saturation) tracking the **full delayed braid field** (causal roots, full branch weight) at the anchored-oblique complex (§72 baseTilt, transport gate open), rather than a prescribed $\gamma/(\gamma+i\omega)$ susceptibility. Central solver `AbsoluteHistoryRootRuntime.mjs` untouched; runner + fixture only; owner suite $47/47$. Fail-closed.

**Result — nonlinearity/saturation buys $\approx6\%$, not $\approx10\times$.** The nonlinear rotator drain over the linear Debye drain (both integrated on the identical delayed field, coupling cancels): multiple $= \{0.98,1.02,1.03,0.97,1.06\}$ across $\gamma/\omega=\{0.25,0.5,1,2,4\}$ — **max $1.06$**. Anchored to the sea's **best-case** linear inner drain (untilted §67, $0.0136$, the strongest it offers anywhere — over-crediting the sea, since the tilted drain is smaller), the native drain is $0.0136\times1.06 = \mathbf{0.0145}$ against the transported target $\approx0.0445$: **fails by $3\times$**, `clearsShortfall=false`. The $R_\perp$ witness (the native drain fed to the reduced complex escapement) still **runs away** (disperses at $t\approx9.0$ — the coherent expansion survives). The conservative guard confirms the drain is genuinely **dissipative**: at $\gamma\to\infty$ (instantaneous tracking, no lag) the drain collapses ($\approx0.88$ peak $\to 0.055$) — no lag, no secular transfer (the §74 lesson, re-confirmed).

**Why (the structural reason the caveat is now sealed).** A saturable orientational dipole is a **bounded unit-vector rotator**; its dissipative lag $\sin^2\delta \le 1$ is capped, and the drain $\gamma\langle\sin^2\delta\rangle$ peaks at $\gamma\sim\omega$ with the same value the linear single-pole χ″ already gives — precisely the band §67/§73 measure. Saturation can only **cap** the response (bound the lag), never enhance it an order of magnitude; nonlinearity on a stiff orientational response cannot buy $10\times$. The §70–§74 arc's linear-estimate caveat is therefore **resolved**: the $\approx10\times$ χ″ shortfall is a **structural bound**, not a linear-estimate artifact.

**Verdict — `native_saturated_chi_below_shortfall_local_sink_nogo_sealed_at_native_grade`.** Door (a) closes as expected: the native nonlinear/saturable sea χ″ falls $3$–$15\times$ short of the transported deficit (best-case to tilted anchor), $R_\perp$ does not flatten, and S1/S2 does **not** close in the rigid + deformation + dynamical-neutral-sea ontology. The local-sink no-go, which the §70–§74 arc established at seed grade, is now sealed above it. Claim level: **native-grade nonlinear dissipative response** — above the linear χ″ (the arc's basis), self-consistent on the full delayed force law, but with the braid worldline still prescribed, so below a full retained-history *release* (the last remaining ceiling; the bounded-rotator structural bound makes a full release unlikely to overturn it, but that is stated as expectation, not proof). Fail-closed: `retainedBranchClaim=false`, `scoreMovement=no_score_increase`; no release, no new schema.

The whole §70–§75 arc is the fail-closed predecessor that makes the radiation reframe necessary rather than optional.

## 76. The (b) Reframe Scoped: the Residual Is the Far-Field Outgoing-Wave Angular-Momentum Flux, Booked as a Continuity Balance — the Light-Cylinder Pin Is the Leading Spin-Down Resolution; Build Spec Written (2026-07-11)

Delivering the Section 75 handoff (operator-directed): the (b) radiation-reaction / outgoing-wake angular-momentum ledger is scoped in a new packet, [radiation-reaction-outgoing-wake-ledger-spec.md](radiation-reaction-outgoing-wake-ledger-spec.md). No new instrument is run here; this books the reframe the §70–§75 no-go forces. Fail-closed.

**The reframe.** With every local near-field sink barred/starved above seed grade (§70–§75), the un-absorbed middle rail pump residual cannot be a local sink — it must be the **outgoing-wave angular-momentum flux the steady braid radiates to the far field**, the one channel every prior instrument excluded by using a bounded near-field environment.

**The ledger (continuity, not force balance).** $dS_{\rm braid}/dt = \dot S_{\rm pump} - \dot S_{\rm self} - \Phi_\infty = 0$ in steady state: near-field pump source $\dot S_{\rm pump}=+0.2274$; internal recirculation (self-hit) $\dot S_{\rm self}=+0.1517$ ($2/3$); far-field wake flux $\Phi_\infty$ the true sink, which steady state requires $=+0.076$ — the residual reframed as a **throughput current** (near field → far field → infinity), not an accumulation. The Row-7 coherent expansion was the rigid release **trapping** this current instead of radiating it.

**The crux (three open questions).** (1) Is the far-field radiation reaction a **brake** of magnitude $\approx0.076$ — exactly cancelling the near-field anti-damping pump residual (then the braid is a self-consistent radiating structure and S1/S2 closes with no environment)? (2) The **spin-down reconciliation** — leading candidate: the **light-cylinder pin**, the rail pinned at $\beta_M=1$ (the field-speed boundary between bound and radiating near/far field, the §11–12 two-sided attractor) sits exactly where the **net** far-field flux is zero, so the field-speed pin is *also* the no-spin-down condition and the $+0.076$ is a reactive near-field bookkeeping term; alternatives are the dissipative-soliton flow and radiative detailed balance with the ambient bath (entry 32 "respiration" at the far-field level). (3) The **secular guard** (the §74/§75 lesson at the far-field sphere): isolate the traveling-wave (radiated, secular) part of $\Phi_\infty$ from the reactive (bound, zero-net) part.

**What building takes.** The decisive measurable ($\Phi_\infty(r)$ through spheres) needs the theory's field **momentum-flux density** — the one genuinely new piece of machinery, a far-field post-processor on the emitted wake (central solver untouched). A cheap **feasibility proxy** exists first: the net self-torque vs **memory depth** — extend the self-hit causal-root sum's look-back to farther (earlier) emission times (the braid's own wake at larger emission radii = its far-field self-interaction) and read whether the net self-torque crosses from anti-damping ($+0.076$, near field only) toward a brake (far-field radiation reaction included). Runner-only, low burden.

**Connections.** S1/S2 closes iff $\Phi_\infty=+0.076$ as a genuine far-field sink; the axis anchor's flutter is booked as trapped-vs-radiated axial wake on the same ledger; and $A_0$ (the mass map) consumes the self-consistent radiating solution — "the speed pin is the size pin" (§58) joined by "the radiation pin is the mass pin," the absolute scale the scale-invariant near-field ledgers cannot set. Claim level: the continuity balance is exact; the residual$=\Phi_\infty$ identification and the light-cylinder resolution are the hypotheses to test.

Next closure goal: run the feasibility proxy — extend the self-hit causal-root sum's memory depth (runner only, central solver untouched, §66 near-field regression green) and measure whether the net self-torque turns from the $+0.076$ near-field anti-damping toward a far-field brake as deeper (farther-emission) wake is included; a sign change re-opens S1/S2 closure within the existing ontology via radiation, persistence hands to the field-momentum-flux build and the light-cylinder-pin fork.

## 78. The Field-Momentum-Flux Build: the Far-Field Angular-Momentum Flux VANISHES ($\Phi_\infty\sim r^{-2.5}$, ~5000x Below the Residual) — the Braid Field Is BOUND, the Light-Cylinder Pin Holds, the +0.076 Is Reactive, and the Sink Question Dissolves (no Net Source to Sink) (2026-07-11)

The electric integrator omits the $R\sin\theta$ lever arm and its causal-root iteration is not converged (residual $\sim0.315$ at $r=256$). The *direction* of the electric conclusion (a velocity/scalar-gradient field with no acceleration term is bound) SURVIVES the corrected recompute — §82 measures the electric/branch channel bound as a **stable vanishing flux** and a reported **slope interval** (not the "$-2.0$ converged" single number, and not this section's $\Phi_\infty\sim r^{-2.5}$) — and §82 additionally measures the magnetic/antisymmetric channel bound from the canonical $W^{\mathrm{rec}}$ force, so the braid is bound on **both** channels (it does not radiate). But this section's inferential CHAIN — "vanishing far flux ⇒ zero net self-torque ⇒ reactive residual ⇒ no sink" — is NOT load-bearing: §82's honest whole-braid net secular z-torque is $+0.424$ (a held-seed diagnostic), not zero, so "the residual is reactive / there is no net source" is unsupported. Bound-on-both-channels does not by itself close S1/S2. See §82.

Executing the Section 77 next closure goal — the decisive far-field measurement (the radiation-reaction packet's measurable 1). New probe `farFieldAngularMomentumFlux` (CLI `--far-field-flux`) computes the braid's retarded field $\mathbf E(X,t)$ (the master-equation **velocity field**: $\sum_s \kappa q_s (1/D_s)\,\hat{\mathbf r}/r^2$, $D_s=c_f-\mathbf v_s\cdot\hat{\mathbf r}$ — the force law's branch weight depends only on velocities; there is **no** acceleration/$1/r$ radiation term) on spheres of increasing radius, and integrates the Maxwell-stress angular-momentum flux $\Phi_\infty(r)=\langle\oint r\,E_\phi E_r\,dA\rangle$, cycle-averaged. Declared stress tensor: electric-type $T_{ij}=E_iE_j-\tfrac12\delta_{ij}E^2$. Central solver untouched; a far-field post-processor on the emitted field; runner only; owner suite $51/51$. Fail-closed; seed grade.

**Result — the far-field flux vanishes; the field is bound.** $|\Phi_\infty(r)|$ over $r\in\{16,32,64,128,256\}$ falls to $\mathbf{1.6\times10^{-5}}$ at $r=256$ — a robust endpoint slope $\mathbf{-2.53}$ (LSQ $-2.82$; radiation would be $\approx0$, an $r$-independent flux) and $\mathbf{2\times10^{-4}}$ of the residual (a radiation sink carrying the $+0.076$ would hold $\Phi_\infty\approx0.076$ constant at every $r$). No radiation component emerges even at $r=256$ ($128\to256$ slope $-2.9$, no flattening toward $0$). The braid's velocity field carries **no net angular momentum to the far field** — it is a bound $\sim1/r^2$ field, exactly as a force law with no radiation term must be. (Declared: intermediate-$r$ quadrature noise on the cancellation-prone $\langle E_\phi E_r\rangle$ integral; the verdict rests on the robust aggregates — the endpoint slope and the flux/residual ratio — not on pointwise slopes.)

**Consequence — the sink question dissolves.** A bound (non-radiating) field carries no angular momentum to infinity, so by conservation the braid's **net secular self-torque is zero** ($\lim_{r\to\infty}\Phi_\infty=0$): there is no far-field sink **because there is no net source to sink**. The $+0.076$ middle-rail residual is therefore **reactive** — a local redistributive term within a globally angular-momentum-conserving bound structure — and the field-speed pin ($\beta_M=1$, the light cylinder) is *also* the **no-spin-down condition** (Crux 2.1 confirmed): the braid sits exactly at the bound/radiating boundary and does not radiate net. The whole §70–§75 local-sink hunt was seeking a sink for what §78 shows is not a net source; the reframe (§76) correctly relocated the question, and the answer is that the question dissolves.

**What this makes of S1/S2.** Closure is no longer "find a sink" but "the bound structure conserves angular momentum internally; the middle rail's local $+0.076$ redistributes to the inner/axis sector via the §72 transport channel (which **opens** at the anchored-oblique complex, ctrRel up to $0.59$), with no environment sink required." The Row-7 coherent expansion was the near-field-truncated rigid release mistaking the reactive residual for a real drive; with the full bound field accounted, there is no net secular source driving it. The remaining step to full closure is the **internal** balance: is the $+0.076$ genuinely reactive (zero net internal work, a bounded redistribution) or does it still drive an internal shape instability — the bound-field internal angular-momentum/energy balance, now freed of any sink requirement.

**Caveats (declared, load-bearing).** (i) The electric-type stress tensor is a declared choice; the theory's exact field stress-energy is not independently established here. (ii) The master equation notes a **history-generated "magnetic-like antisymmetric response"** ([master-equation.md](../../../../content/markdown/aaa/dynamics/master-equation.md)); this velocity-field computation does **not** include any radiation that channel might carry — it is the one open door above this grade, and if it carries far-field flux the bound verdict weakens. (iii) Seed grade, prescribed rigid worldline; quadrature noise as declared. Claim level: the velocity-field flux vanishing is **measured** (robust aggregates); "bound $\Rightarrow$ zero net secular self-torque $\Rightarrow$ reactive residual $\Rightarrow$ no sink needed" is **derivation from conservation**; full S1/S2 closure via internal redistribution is the **reduced remaining target**. Fail-closed: `retainedBranchClaim=false`, `scoreMovement=no_score_increase`.
